.

Theorem 31
.

∗
Let
\textbf{{S}}_{tring}(\leq n)
be the set of
m
-rank number-based strings

\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\cdots\alpha_{m}
with each number
\alpha_{j}\geq 0
for
j\in[1,m]
and
m\leq n
. Suppose that a connected
(p,q)
-graph
H
admits
n
proper total colorings
h_{1},h_{2}
,
\dots
,
h_{n}
such that each total coloring
h_{i}
obeys a
W_{i}
-constraint
h_{i}(uv)=W_{i}\langle h_{i}(u),h_{i}(v)\rangle
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
and
i\in[1,n]
, then this graph
G
admits a
\{W_{i}\}^{n}_{i=1}
-constraint
no-order homogeneous proper total string-coloring

\varphi:V(H)\cup E(H)\rightarrow\textbf{{S}}_{tring}(\leq n)
defined by

{\begin{split}\varphi(u)&=h_{i_{1}}(u)h_{i_{2}}(u)\cdots h_{i_{n}}(u),~\varphi(uv)=h_{j_{1}}(uv)h_{j_{2}}(uv)\cdots h_{j_{n}}(uv),\\
\varphi(v)&=h_{k_{1}}(v)h_{k_{2}}(v)\cdots h_{k_{n}}(v)\end{split}}

(92)

where the
vertex string

h_{i_{1}}(u)h_{i_{2}}(u)\cdots h_{i_{n}}(u)
is a permutation of vertex colors
h_{1}(u),h_{2}(u)
,
\dots
,
h_{n}(u)
, and the
edge string

h_{j_{1}}(uv)h_{j_{2}}(uv)\cdots h_{j_{n}}(uv)
is a permutation of edge colors
h_{1}(uv),h_{2}(uv)
,
\dots
,
h_{n}(uv)
, and the
vertex string

h_{k_{1}}(v)h_{k_{2}}(v)\cdots h_{k_{n}}(v)
is a permutation of vertex colors
h_{1}(v)
,
h_{2}(v)
,
\dots
,
h_{n}(v)
; such that

(i)
\varphi(u)\neq\varphi(v)
,
\varphi(u)\neq\varphi(uv)
and
\varphi(uv)\neq\varphi(v)
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
;

(ii)
\varphi(uv)\neq\varphi(uw)
for any pair of adjacent edges
uv,uw\in E(H)
;

(iii) each edge number
h_{j_{c}}(uv)
corresponds two vertex numbers
h_{i_{a}}(u)
and
h_{k_{b}}(v)
holding the
W_{j_{c}}
-constraint
h_{j_{c}}(uv)=W_{j_{c}}\langle h_{i_{a}}(u),h_{k_{b}}(v)\rangle
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
;

(iv) each vertex number
h_{i_{a}}(u)
corresponds two numbers
h_{j_{c}}(uv)
and
h_{k_{b}}(v)
holding the
W_{i_{a}}
-constraint
h_{j_{c}}(uv)=W_{i_{a}}\langle h_{i_{a}}(u),h_{k_{b}}(v)\rangle
for each vertex
u\in V(H)
.

(v) each vertex number
h_{k_{b}}(v)
corresponds two numbers
h_{j_{c}}(uv)
and
h_{i_{a}}(u)
holding the
W_{k_{b}}
-constraint
h_{j_{c}}(uv)=W_{k_{b}}\langle h_{i_{a}}(u),h_{k_{b}}(v)\rangle
for each vertex
v\in V(H)
.

Theorem 32
.

∗
Let
\textbf{{S}}_{tring}(\leq n)
be the set of
m
-rank number-based strings

\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\cdots\alpha_{m}
with each number
\alpha_{j}\geq 0
for
j\in[1,m]
and
m\leq n
. Suppose that a connected
(p,q)
-graph
T
admits
n
proper total colorings
g_{1},g_{2}
,
\dots
,
g_{n}
such that each total coloring
g_{i}
obeys a
W_{i}
-constraint
g_{i}(uv)=W_{i}\langle g_{i}(u),g_{i}(v)\rangle
for each edge
uv\in E(T)
and
i\in[1,n]
, then this graph
G
admits a
\{W_{i}\}^{n}_{i=1}
-constraint
non-homogeneous proper total string-coloring

\theta:V(T)\cup E(T)\rightarrow\textbf{{S}}_{tring}(\leq n)
defined by

{\begin{split}\theta(u)&=g_{i_{1}}(u)g_{i_{2}}(u)\cdots g_{i_{A}}(u),~\theta(uv)=g_{j_{1}}(uv)g_{j_{2}}(uv)\cdots g_{j_{B}}(uv),\\
\theta(v)&=g_{k_{1}}(v)g_{k_{2}}(v)\cdots g_{k_{C}}(v)\end{split}}

(93)

with
1\leq A,B,C\leq n
, where the
vertex string

g_{i_{1}}(u)g_{i_{2}}(u)\cdots g_{i_{A}}(u)
is a sub-permutation of vertex colors
g_{1}(u),g_{2}(u)
,
\dots
,
g_{n}(u)
, and the
edge string

g_{j_{1}}(uv)g_{j_{2}}(uv)\cdots g_{j_{B}}(uv)
is a sub-permutation of edge colors
g_{1}(uv),g_{2}(uv)
,
\dots
,
g_{n}(uv)
, and the
vertex string

g_{k_{1}}(v)g_{k_{2}}(v)\cdots g_{k_{C}}(v)
is a sub-permutation of vertex colors
g_{1}(v)
,
g_{2}(v)
,
\dots
,
g_{n}(v)
; such that

(i)
\theta(u)\neq\theta(v)
,
\theta(u)\neq\theta(uv)
and
\theta(uv)\neq\theta(v)
for each edge
uv\in E(T)
;

(ii)
\theta(uv)\neq\theta(uw)
for any pair of adjacent edges
uv,uw\in E(T)
;

(iii) each edge number
g_{j_{s}}(uv)
corresponds two vertex numbers
g_{i_{r}}(u)
and
g_{k_{t}}(v)
holding the
W_{j_{s}}
-constraint
g_{j_{s}}(uv)=W_{j_{s}}\langle g_{i_{r}}(u),g_{k_{t}}(v)\rangle
for each edge
uv\in E(T)
;

(iv) each vertex number
g_{i_{r}}(u)
corresponds two numbers
g_{j_{s}}(uv)
and
g_{k_{t}}(v)
holding the
W_{i_{r}}
-constraint
g_{j_{s}}(uv)=W_{i_{r}}\langle g_{i_{r}}(u),g_{k_{t}}(v)\rangle
for each vertex
u\in V(T)
.

(v) each vertex number
g_{k_{t}}(v)
corresponds two numbers
g_{j_{s}}(uv)
and
g_{i_{r}}(u)
holding the
W_{k_{t}}
-constraint
g_{j_{s}}(uv)=W_{k_{t}}\langle g_{i_{r}}(u),g_{k_{t}}(v)\rangle
for each vertex
v\in V(T)
.

4
Graph-based strings

4.1
Total graph-colorings

Definition 28
.

[
37
]
Let
G_{x,i},G_{e,j}
and
G_{y,k}
be colored graphs with
1\leq i,j,k\leq q
. A
graphic Topcode-matrix

G_{code}
is defined as

\centering{\begin{split}G_{code}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}G_{x,1}&G_{x,2}&\cdots&G_{x,q}\\
G_{e,1}&G_{e,2}&\cdots&G_{e,q}\\
G_{y,1}&G_{y,2}&\cdots&G_{y,q}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}G_{X}\\
G_{E}\\
G_{Y}\end{array}\right)=(G_{X},~G_{E},~G_{Y})^{T}\end{split}}\@add@centering

(94)

both colored graphs
G_{x,i}
and
G_{y,i}
are the
ends
of the colored graph
G_{e,i}
, and we call

G_{X}=(G_{x,1},G_{x,2},\dots,G_{x,q}),~G_{E}=(G_{e,1},G_{e,2},\dots,G_{e,q}),~G_{Y}=(G_{y,1},G_{y,2},\dots,G_{y,q})

graphic vectors
.

Moreover the graphic Topcode-matrix
G_{code}
is
constraint valued
if there is a group of constraints
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\dots,\varphi_{m}
such that
G_{e,i}=\varphi_{j}\langle G_{x,i},G_{y,i}\rangle
for some
j\in[1,m]
and each
i\in[1,q]
.
\square

Definition 29
.

∗
If a number-based string
s(n)
can be made by some colored graph, then we call it
topological number-based string
, and the colored graph is a
topological expression
of the number-based string
s(n)
.
\square

Remark 18
.

In Definition
29
, a topological number-based string can be expressed by two or more topological expressions.
\parallel

Let
\textbf{{T}}_{i}=\big(T_{i,1},T_{i,2},\dots,T_{i,n_{i}}\big)
be a
colored graph base
for
n_{i}\geq 1
and
i\in[1,M]
, and each colored graph
T_{i,j}
admits a total coloring (or a total graph-coloring)
f_{i,j}
for
j\in[1,n_{i}]
. Suppose that each colored graph base
\textbf{{T}}_{i}
is an
every-zero additive group
defined in Definition
67
.

Definition 30
.

∗
A new Abelian additive operation is defined as

T_{i,r}~[\ominus\oplus_{k}]~T_{i,j}:=T_{i,k}\ominus T_{i,r}\oplus T_{i,j}=T_{i,\mu}\in\textbf{{T}}_{i}

(95)

with the index
\mu=k-(r+j)~(\bmod~M)
for any preappointed
zero

T_{i,k}\in\textbf{{T}}_{i}
. So, we get an
every-zero edge-index graphic group

\{F^{-}_{M}(\textbf{{T}}_{i});\ominus\oplus\}
of order
M
defined by the new Abelian operation
T_{i,r}~[\ominus\oplus_{k}]~T_{i,j}
based on a colored graph base
\textbf{{T}}_{i}
.
\square

Figure 16:

An edge-index graphic group
\{F^{-}_{6}(G);\ominus\oplus\}
based on
G\{T_{1},T_{2},\dots,T_{6}\}
for understanding Definition
30
.

Definition 31
.

∗
A
(p,q)
-graph
H
admits an
edge-index total graph-coloring

\theta:V(H)\cup E(H)\rightarrow\textbf{{T}}_{i}=\big\{T_{i,1},T_{i,2},\dots,T_{i,n_{i}}\big\}
, such that each edge
uv\in E(H)
holds
F(u)=T_{i,r}
,
F(v)=T_{i,j}
and
F(uv)=T_{i,\lambda}
with the index
\lambda=r+j-k~(\bmod~M)
under the Abelian additive operation

T_{i,r}~[\oplus\ominus_{k}]~T_{i,j}:=T_{i,r}\oplus T_{i,j}\ominus T_{i,k}=T_{i,\lambda}\in\textbf{{T}}_{i}

(96)

for any preappointed
zero

T_{i,k}\in\textbf{{T}}_{i}
, where
M
is a positive integer. Let

F_{\textrm{e-index}}(E(H))=\{s=r+j-k~(\bmod~M):~\theta(uv)=T_{i,s},uv\in E(H)\}

be the
edge-index set
for
\theta(u)=T_{i,r}
,
\theta(v)=T_{i,j}
and
\theta(uv)=T_{i,s}
. Let
\lambda
be a non-negative integer.
Then we call
\theta
:

Incol
-1.

an
edge-index graceful total graph-coloring
if
F_{\textrm{e-index}}(E(H))=[1,q]
holds true.

Incol
-2.

an
edge-index odd-graceful total graph-coloring
if
F_{\textrm{e-index}}(E(H))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
holds true.

Incol
-3.

an
edge-index edge-difference total graph-coloring
if the edge-difference constraint
s+|r-j|=\lambda
holds true.

Incol
-4.

an
edge-index felicitous-difference total graph-coloring
if the felicitous-difference constraint
|r+j-s|=\lambda
holds true.

Incol
-5.

an
edge-index graceful-difference total graph-coloring
if the graceful-difference constraint
\big||r+j|-s\big|=\lambda
holds true.

Incol
-6.

an
edge-index edge-magic total graph-coloring
if the edge-magic constraint
s+r+j=\lambda
holds true.
\square

Example 21
.

In Fig.
17
,
F(uv)=T_{ij}
,
F(u)=T_{i}
and
F(v)=T_{j}
. So, we have

(a) An odd-graceful edge-index felicitous-difference total graph-coloring holding the felicitous-difference constraint
|i+j-ij|=6~(\bmod~12)
;

(b) an odd-graceful edge-index graceful-difference total graph-coloring holding the graceful-difference constraint
\big||i-j|-ij\big|=0~(\bmod~12)
, and each vertex of (a) is colored the same color with the image vertex of (b);

(c) an odd-graceful edge-index edge-magic total graph-coloring holding the edge-magic constraint
i+j+ij=4~(\bmod~12)
under
4-(i+j)=ij~(\bmod~12)
, and each edge index of (c) (as a
public-key
) matches with edge index of the image edge of (b) (as a
private-key
), such that the sum of two edge indices is equal to 12;

(d) an odd-graceful edge-index felicitous-difference total graph-coloring holding the felicitous-difference constraint
|i+j-ij|=4~(\bmod~12)
;

(e) an odd-graceful edge-index felicitous-difference total graph-coloring holding the felicitous-difference constraint
|i+j-ij|=4~(\bmod~12)
, where each vertex index of (b) matches with vertex index of the image edge of (e), such that the sum of two vertex indices is equal to 15;

(f) each edge-graph
F(uv)=T_{ij}
corresponds to another edge-graph
F(xy)=T_{xy}
, such that
i+j=xy~(\bmod~12)
.
\square

.

Example 22
.

In Fig.
18
,
F(uv)=T_{ij}
,
F(u)=T_{i}
and
F(v)=T_{j}
, we have

(a) A graceful edge-index felicitous-difference total graph-coloring holding the felicitous-difference constraint
|i+j-ij|=5~(\bmod~6)
;

(b) a graceful edge-index edge-difference total graph-coloring holding the edge-difference constraint
ij+|i-j|=7~(\bmod~6)
;

(c) a graceful edge-index felicitous-difference total graph-coloring holding the felicitous-difference constraint
|i+j-ij|=4~(\bmod~6)
;

(d) an edge-index felicitous-difference total graph-coloring holding the graceful-difference constraint
\big||i-j|-ij\big|=3~(\bmod~6)
;

(e) a graceful edge-index felicitous-difference total graph-coloring holding the felicitous-difference constraint
|i+j-ij|=5~(\bmod~6)
;

(f) a graceful edge-index felicitous-difference total graph-coloring holding the felicitous-difference constraint
|i+j-ij|=2~(\bmod~6)
;

i+j+ij=10~(\bmod~6)
, where each edge-graph
F(uv)=T_{ij}
corresponds to another edge-graph
F(xy)=T_{xy}
with
i+j-6~(\bmod~6)=xy
;

(h) a graceful edge-index felicitous-difference total graph-coloring with
i+j+ij=12~(\bmod~6)
, where
k-(i+j)=ij~(\bmod~7)
.
\square

4.2
TOTAL-graph-coloring algorithm-I

We show the TOTAL-graph-coloring algorithms for producing
t
-rank topological number-based strings
in this subsection.

TOTAL-graph-coloring algorithm-I.

Initialization.
By Definition
31
, a
(p,q)
-graph
H
has its own Topcode-matrix

T_{code}\big(H,F^{1}_{0}\big)=\big(X^{1}_{0},E^{1}_{0},Y^{1}_{0}\big)^{T}

(97)

with the vertex vector
X^{1}_{0}=\big(F^{1}_{0}\big(x^{0}_{1}\big)
,
F^{1}_{0}\big(x^{0}_{2}\big),\dots,F^{1}_{0}\big(x^{0}_{q}\big)\big)
, the edge vector
E^{1}_{0}=\big(F^{1}_{0}\big(x^{0}_{1}y^{0}_{1}\big)
,
F^{1}_{0}\big(x^{0}_{2}y^{0}_{2}\big)
,
\dots,F^{1}_{0}\big(x^{0}_{q}y^{0}_{q}\big)\big)
and the vertex vector
Y^{1}_{0}=\big(F^{1}_{0}\big(y^{0}_{1}\big),F^{1}_{0}\big(y^{0}_{2}\big),\dots,F^{1}_{0}\big(y^{0}_{q}\big)\big)
under an
edge-index total graph-coloring

F^{1}_{0}
defined in Definition
31
. For the simplicity of statement, we write

U^{0}(H)=X^{1}_{0}\cup E^{1}_{0}\cup Y^{1}_{0}=\big\{F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{1}\big),F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{2}\big),\dots,F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{3q}\big)\big\}

(98)

and we get a
graph-based string

S^{3q}_{k_{0}}(H)=F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{j_{1}}\big)F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{j_{2}}\big)\cdots F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{j_{3q}}\big),~k_{0}\in[1,(3q)!]

(99)

where
F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{j_{1}}\big),F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{j_{2}}\big),\cdots,F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{j_{3q}}\big)
is a permutation of elements of the graph set
U^{0}(H)
defined in Eq.(
98
).

Since each graph
F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{j_{s}}\big)=T_{1,k_{j_{s}}}\in\textbf{{T}}_{1}
for
s\in[1,3q]
and
1\leq k_{j_{s}}\leq n_{1}
, then the graph
T_{1,k_{j_{s}}}
has its own Topcode-matrix

T_{code}\big(T_{1,k_{j_{s}}},f_{1,k_{j_{s}}}\big)=T_{code}\big(F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{j_{s}}\big),f_{1,k_{j_{s}}}\big)=\big(X_{1,k_{j_{s}}},E_{1,k_{j_{s}}},Y_{1,k_{j_{s}}}\big)^{T}

(100)

under the total coloring

(i)
f_{1,k_{j_{s}}}:V(T_{1,k_{j_{s}}})\cup E(T_{1,k_{j_{s}}})\rightarrow Z_{num}
, where
Z_{num}
is an integer set; or

(ii)
f_{1,k_{j_{s}}}:V(T_{1,k_{j_{s}}})\cup E(T_{1,k_{j_{s}}})\rightarrow Z_{grd}
, where
Z_{grd}
is a graph set.

Step 1.
Notice that
F^{1}_{0}(w_{i})=T_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\in\textbf{{T}}_{1}
with
i\in[1,3q_{1}]
and
1\leq k^{1}_{i}\leq n_{1}
, and let
N_{1,i}=|E\big(T_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big)|
. There is the Topcode-matrix

T_{code}\big(T_{1,k^{1}_{i}},f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big)=T_{code}\big(F^{1}_{0}(w_{i}),f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big)=\big(X_{1,k^{1}_{i}},E_{1,k^{1}_{i}},Y_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big)^{T}

(101)

having

its own vertex vector
X_{1,k^{1}_{i}}=\big(f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(x^{1}_{1}\big),f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(x^{1}_{2}\big),\dots,f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(x^{1}_{N_{1,i}}\big)\big)
with
x^{1}_{j}\in V(T_{1,k^{1}_{i}})
for
j\in[1,N_{1,i}]
,

its own edge vector
E_{1,k^{1}_{i}}=\big(f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(x^{1}_{1}y^{1}_{1}\big),f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(x^{1}_{2}y^{1}_{2}\big),\dots,f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(x^{1}_{q_{1}}y^{1}_{N_{1,i}}\big)\big)
with
x^{1}_{j}y^{1}_{j}\in E(T_{1,k^{1}_{i}})
for
j\in[1,N_{1,i}]
, and

its own vertex vector
Y_{1,k^{1}_{i}}=\big(f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(y^{1}_{1}\big),f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(y^{1}_{2}\big),\dots,f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(y^{1}_{N_{1,i}}\big)\big)
with
y^{1}_{j}\in V(T_{1,k^{1}_{i}})
for
j\in[1,N_{1,i}]

under the total coloring

(i)
f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}:V(T_{1,k^{1}_{i}})\cup E(T_{1,k^{1}_{i}})\rightarrow Z_{num}
, where
Z_{num}
is an integer set; or

(ii)
f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}:V(T_{1,k^{1}_{i}})\cup E(T_{1,k^{1}_{i}})\rightarrow Z_{grd}
, where
Z_{grd}
is a graph set.

If the above (ii) holds true, we have a graph set

U^{1}_{1,k^{1}_{i}}(T_{1,k^{1}_{i}})=X_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\cup E_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\cup Y_{1,k^{1}_{i}}=\big\{f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{1}_{i},1}\big),f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{1}_{i},2}\big),\dots,f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{1}_{i},~3N_{1,i}}\big)\big\}

(102)

Moreover, we get a graph-based string

S^{N_{1,i}}_{k_{1,i}}(T_{1,k^{1}_{i}})=f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{1}_{i},j_{1}}\big)f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{1}_{i},j_{2}}\big)\cdots f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{1}_{i},~j_{3N_{1,i}}}\big)=S^{N_{1,j_{1}}N_{1,j_{2}}\cdots N_{1,j_{3N_{1,i}}}}_{k_{1,j_{1}}k_{1,j_{2}}\cdots k_{1,j_{3N_{1,i}}}}

(103)

with
k_{1,i}\in[1,(3N_{1,i})!]
, where
f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{1}_{i},j_{1}}\big),f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{1}_{i},j_{2}}\big),\dots,f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{1}_{i},~j_{3N_{1,i}}}\big)
is a permutation of elements of the graph set
U^{2}_{1,k^{1}_{i}}(T_{1,k^{1}_{i}})
defined in Eq.(
102
).

By Eq.(
99
) and Eq.(
103
), we have

S^{3q}_{k_{0}}(H)=F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{j_{1}}\big)F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{j_{2}}\big)\cdots F^{1}_{0}\big(w_{j_{3q}}\big)=S^{N_{1,j_{1}}N_{1,j_{2}}\cdots N_{1,j_{3q}}}_{k_{1,j_{1}}k_{1,j_{2}}\cdots k_{1,j_{3q}}}

(104)

Step 2.
Since each graph
f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{1}_{i},~i}\big)=T_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\in\textbf{{T}}_{2}
with
i\in[1,3N_{1,i}]
and
1\leq k^{2}_{i}\leq n_{2}
, and let
N_{2,i}=|E\big(T_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big)|
. We get the Topcode-matrix

T_{code}\big(T_{2,k^{2}_{i}},f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big)=T_{code}\big(f_{1,k^{1}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{1}_{i},~i}\big),f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big)=\big(X_{2,k^{2}_{i}},E_{2,k^{2}_{i}},Y_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big)^{T}

(105)

having

its own vertex vector
X_{2,k^{2}_{i}}=\big(f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big(x^{2}_{1}\big),f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big(x^{2}_{2}\big),\dots,f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big(x^{2}_{N_{2,i}}\big)\big)
with
x^{2}_{j}\in V(T_{2,k^{2}_{i}})
for
j\in[1,N_{2,i}]
,

its own edge vector
E_{2,k^{2}_{i}}=\big(f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big(x^{2}_{1}y^{2}_{1}\big),f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big(x^{2}_{2}y^{2}_{2}\big),\dots,f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big(x^{2}_{q_{1}}y^{2}_{N_{2,i}}\big)\big)
with
x^{2}_{j}y^{2}_{j}\in E(T_{2,k^{2}_{i}})
for
j\in[1,N_{2,i}]
,

its own vertex vector
Y_{2,k^{2}_{i}}=\big(f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big(y^{2}_{1}\big),f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big(y^{2}_{2}\big),\dots,f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big(y^{2}_{N_{2,i}}\big)\big)
with
y^{2}_{j}\in V(T_{2,k^{2}_{i}})
for
j\in[1,N_{2,i}]

under the total coloring

(i)
f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}:V(T_{2,k^{2}_{i}})\cup E(T_{2,k^{2}_{i}})\rightarrow Z_{num}
, where
Z_{num}
is an integer set; or

(ii)
f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}:V(T_{2,k^{2}_{i}})\cup E(T_{2,k^{2}_{i}})\rightarrow Z_{grd}
, where
Z_{grd}
is a graph set.

If the above (ii) holds true, by

U^{2}_{2,k^{2}_{i}}(T_{2,k^{2}_{i}})=X_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\cup E_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\cup Y_{2,k^{2}_{i}}=\big\{f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{2}_{i},1}\big),f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{2}_{i},2}\big),\dots,f_{2,k^{2}_{i}}\big(w_{k^{2}_{i},~3N_{2,i}}\big)\big\}

(106)

we get a graph-based string

4.3
TOTAL-graph-coloring algorithm-II

Let
\textbf{{O}}=(O_{1},O_{2},\dots,O_{p})
be a
graph operation base
, where each
O_{i}
is a graph operation of graph theory, and let
\textbf{{T}}_{i}=\big(T_{i,1},T_{i,2},\dots,T_{i,n_{i}}\big)
be a
colored graph base
for
n_{i}\geq 1
and
i\in[1,M]
, so that each colored graph
T_{i,j}
admits a total coloring (or a total graph-coloring)
f_{i,j}
. Suppose that each colored graph base
\textbf{{T}}_{i}
is an every-zero additive group defined in Definition
67
.

TOTAL-graph-coloring algorithm-II with graph operation.

Input.
A
(p_{1},q_{1})
-graph
H_{1}
admits an
edge-index total graph-coloring

F_{1}
defined in Definition
31
.

Output.

t
-rank topological number-based strings.

Step-II-1.
A
(p_{1},q_{1})
-graph
H_{1}
admitting an
edge-index total graph-coloring

F_{1}:V(H_{1})\cup E(H_{1})\rightarrow\textbf{{T}}_{1}
, such that each edge
uv\in E(H_{1})
holds
F_{1}(u)=T_{1,i}
,
F_{1}(v)=T_{1,j}
and
F_{1}(uv)=T_{1,\lambda}
with the index
\lambda=i+j-k_{1}~(\bmod~M_{1})
for a preappointed
zero

k_{1}\in[1,n_{1}]
.

Step-II-1.1.
From the edge-index total graph-coloring
F_{1}
of the graph
H_{1}
, we get a Topcode-matrix
T_{code}(H_{1},F_{1})=(X_{1},E_{1},Y_{1})^{T}
with the vertex vector

,
F_{1}(x_{1,2}),\dots,F_{1}(x_{1,q_{1}}))
, the edge vector
E_{1}=(F_{1}(x_{1,1}y_{1,1}),F_{1}(x_{1,2}y_{1,2}),\dots,F_{1}(x_{1,q_{1}}y_{1,q_{1}}))
and the vertex vector
Y_{1}=(F_{1}(y_{1,1}),F_{1}(y_{1,2}),\dots,F_{1}(y_{1,q_{1}}))
. For the simplicity of statement, we write

U(H_{1})=X_{1}\cup E_{1}\cup Y_{1}=\{F_{1}(w_{1,1}),F_{1}(w_{1,2}),\dots,F_{1}(w_{1,3q_{1}})\}

(119)

and we get
graph-based strings

with

Step-II-1.2.
Since each graph

and

.

We get a graph set

{\begin{split}U(T_{1,(r,j)})&=X_{1,(r,j)}\cup E_{1,(r,j)}\cup Y_{1,(r,j)}\\
&=\{f_{1,(r,j)}(z_{1,(r,j),1}),f_{1,(r,j)}(z_{1,(r,j),3}),\dots,f_{1,(r,j)}(z_{1,(r,j),3q(1,r,j)})\}\end{split}}

(121)

with

Each graph

distributes us a topological number-based string

then each graph-based string

defined in Eq.(

) distributes us a

-rank topological number-based string

(122)

with

.

Step-II-1.3.
A
(p_{2},q_{2})
-graph
H_{2}
is obtained in the following way: For each edge

, do a graph operation
O_{s}\in\textbf{{O}}
to the graphs

and

for producing a subgraph

F_{1}(v)
and
F_{1}(uv)
for producing a subgraph

. We write

for

and

, immediately, we get an
operation graphic lattice

\textbf{{L}}(\textbf{{Q}}_{1}[\textbf{{O}}]\textbf{{T}}_{1})=\left\{H[\textbf{{O}}]^{n_{1}}_{k=1}a_{1,k}T_{1,k}:~a_{1,k}\in Z^{0},~T_{1,k}\in\textbf{{T}}_{1},H\in\textbf{{Q}}_{1}\right\}

(123)

with
\sum^{n_{1}}_{k=1}a_{1,k}\geq 1
, where

is a graph set.

Step-II-2.
Suppose the
(p_{2},q_{2})
-graph
H_{2}
admits an

,

and

\lambda=i+j-k_{2}~(\bmod~M_{2})
for a preappointed
zero

T_{2,k_{2}}\in\textbf{{T}}_{2}
; refer to Definition
31
.

Step-II-2.1.
From the edge-index total graph-coloring
F_{2}
of the graph
H_{2}
, we get a Topcode-matrix
T_{code}(H_{2},F_{2})=(X_{2},E_{2},Y_{2})^{T}
with the vertex vector

,

, the edge vector

and the vertex vector
Y_{2}=(F_{2}(y_{2,1}),F_{2}(y_{2,2}),\dots,F_{2}(y_{2,q_{2}}))

U(H_{2})=X_{2}\cup E_{2}\cup Y_{2}=\{F_{2}(w_{2,r,1}),F_{1}(w_{2,r,2}),\cdots,F_{1}(w_{2,r,3q_{2}})\}

(124)

and we get a
graph-based string

S_{gb}(H_{2},r)=F_{2}(w_{2,r,1})F_{1}(w_{2,r,2})\cdots F_{1}(w_{2,r,3q_{2}})

(125)

with
r\in[1,(3q_{2})!]
, where
F_{2}(w_{2,r,1})F_{1}(w_{2,r,2})\cdots F_{1}(w_{2,r,3q_{2}})
is a permutation of elements of the graph set
U(H_{2})
defined in Eq.(
124
).

Step-II-2.2.
Since each graph
F_{2}(w_{2,r,j})=T_{2,(r,j)}\in\textbf{{T}}_{2}
with
j\in[1,3q_{2}]
, and
T_{2,(r,j)}
admits a a total coloring
f_{2,(r,j)}:V(T_{2,(r,j)})\cup E(T_{2,(r,j)})\rightarrow W_{2,(r,j)}
, where
W_{2,(r,j)}
is a non-negative integer set. Let
q(2,r,j)=|E(T_{2,(r,j)})|
.

So we have a Topcode-matrix
T_{code}(T_{2,(r,j)},f_{2,(r,j)})=(X_{2,(r,j)},E_{2,(r,j)},Y_{2,(r,j)})^{T}
with its own vertex vectors and edge vector as follows

X_{2,(r,j)}=(f_{2,(r,j)}(u_{2,(r,j),1})
,
f_{2,(r,j)}(u_{2,(r,j),2}),\dots,f_{2,(r,j)}(u_{2,(r,j),q(2,r,j)}))
,

E_{2,(r,j)}=(f_{2,(r,j)}(u_{2,(r,j),1}v_{2,(r,j),1}),f_{2,(r,j)}(u_{2,(r,j),2}v_{2,(r,j),2}),\dots,f_{2,(r,j)}(u_{2,(r,j),q(2,r,j)}v_{2,(r,j),q(2,r,j)}))
,

Y_{2,(r,j)}=(f_{2,(r,j)}(v_{2,(r,j),1}),f_{2,(r,j)}(v_{2,(r,j),2}),\dots,f_{2,(r,j)}(v_{2,(r,j),q(2,r,j)}))
.

We get a graph set

{\begin{split}U(T_{2,(r,j)})&=X_{2,(r,j)}\cup E_{2,(r,j)}\cup Y_{2,(r,j)}\\
&=\{f_{2,(r,j)}(z_{2,(r,j),1}),f_{2,(r,j)}(z_{2,(r,j),3}),\dots,f_{2,(r,j)}(z_{2,(r,j),3q(2,r,j)})\}\end{split}}

(126)

with
r\in[1,(3q_{2})!]
and
j\in[1,3q_{2}]
.

Each graph
F_{2}(w_{2,r,j})=T_{2,(r,j)}\in\textbf{{T}}_{2}
distributes us a topological number-based string

f_{2,(r,j)}(z_{2,(r,j),1})f_{2,(r,j)}(z_{2,(r,j),3})\cdots f_{2,(r,j)}(z_{2,(r,j),3q(2,r,j)})=S^{q(2,r,j)}_{(r,j)}

then each graph-based string
S_{gb}(H_{2},r)
defined in Eq.(
125
) distributes us a
2
-rank topological number-based string
as follows

s(H_{2},r)=S^{q(2,r,1)q(2,r,2)\cdots q(2,r,3q_{2})}_{(r,1)(r,2)\cdots(r,3q_{2})}\leftarrow S_{gb}(H_{2},r)=F_{2}(w_{2,r,1})F_{2}(w_{2,r,2})\cdots F_{2}(w_{2,r,3q_{2}})

(127)

with
r\in[1,(3q_{2})!]
.

Step-II-2.3.
A
(p_{3},q_{3})
-graph
H_{3}
is obtained in the following way: For each edge
xy\in E(H_{2})
, do a graph operation
O_{xy}\in\textbf{{O}}
to the graphs
F_{2}(x)
and
F_{2}(xy)
for producing a subgraph
F_{2}(x)O_{xy}F_{2}(xy)
, and do a graph operation
O_{j}\in\textbf{{O}}
to the graphs
F_{2}(y)
and
F_{2}(xy)
for producing a subgraph
F_{2}(y)O_{j}F_{2}(xy)
, then we get a subgraph (also graph-edge)
F_{2}(x)O_{xy}F_{2}(xy)O_{j}F_{2}(y)
. We write
H_{3}=H_{2}[\textbf{{O}}]^{n_{2}}_{k=1}a_{2,k}T_{2,k}
for
a_{2,k}\in Z^{0}
and
T_{2,k}\in\textbf{{T}}_{2}
.

Let
\textbf{{Q}}_{2}=\textbf{{L}}(\textbf{{Q}}_{1}[\textbf{{O}}]\textbf{{T}}_{1})
defined in Eq.(
123
), we get an
operation graphic lattice

\textbf{{L}}(\textbf{{Q}}_{2}[\textbf{{O}}]\textbf{{T}}_{2})=\big\{H[\textbf{{O}}]^{n_{2}}_{k=1}a_{2,k}T_{2,k}:~a_{2,k}\in Z^{0},~T_{2,k}\in\textbf{{T}}_{2},H\in\textbf{{Q}}_{2}\big\}

(128)

with
\sum^{n_{2}}_{k=1}a_{2,k}\geq 1
.

Step-II-
t
.
Suppose the
(p_{t},q_{t})
-graph
H_{t}
admits an
edge-index total graph-coloring

F_{t}:V(H_{t})\cup E(H_{t})\rightarrow\textbf{{T}}_{t}
, such that each edge
xy\in E(H_{t})
holds
F_{t}(x)=T_{t,i}
,
F_{t}(y)=T_{t,j}
and
F_{t}(xy)=T_{t,\lambda}
with the index
\lambda=i+j-k_{t}~(\bmod~M_{t})
for a preappointed
zero-index

T_{t,k_{t}}\in\textbf{{T}}_{t}
; refer to Definition
31
.

Step-II-
t
.1.
From the edge-index total graph-coloring
F_{t}
of the graph
H_{t}
, we get a Topcode-matrix
T_{code}(H_{t},F_{t})=(X_{t},E_{t},Y_{t})^{T}
with the vertex vector
X_{t}=(F_{t}(x_{t,1})
,
F_{t}(x_{t,2}),\dots,F_{t}(x_{t,q_{t}}))
, the edge vector
E_{t}=(F_{t}(x_{t,1}y_{t,1}),F_{t}(x_{t,2}y_{t,2}),\dots,F_{t}(x_{t,q_{t}}y_{t,q_{t}}))
and
the vertex vector
Y_{t}=(F_{t}(y_{t,1})
,
F_{t}(y_{t,2}),\dots,F_{t}(y_{t,q_{t}}))
. For the simplicity of statement, we write

U(H_{t})=X_{t}\cup E_{t}\cup Y_{t}=\{F_{t}(w_{t,r,1}),F_{t}(w_{t,r,2}),\cdots,F_{t}(w_{t,r,3q_{t}})\}

(129)

and we get a
graph-based string

S_{gb}(H_{t},r)=F_{t}(w_{t,r,1})F_{t}(w_{t,r,2})\cdots F_{t}(w_{t,r,3q_{t}})

(130)

with
r\in[1,(3q_{t})!]
, where
F_{t}(w_{t,r,1})F_{t}(w_{t,r,2})\cdots F_{t}(w_{t,r,3q_{t}})
is a permutation of elements of the graph set
U(H_{t})
defined in Eq.(
129
).

Step-II-
t
.2.
Since each graph
F_{t}(w_{t,r,j})=T_{t,(r,j)}\in\textbf{{T}}_{t}
with
j\in[1,3q_{t}]
, and
T_{t,(r,j)}
admits a a total coloring
f_{t,(r,j)}:V(T_{t,(r,j)})\cup E(T_{t,(r,j)})\rightarrow W_{t,(r,j)}
, where
W_{t,(r,j)}
is a non-negative integer set. Let
q(t,r,j)=|E(T_{t,(r,j)})|
. So we have a Topcode-matrix

T_{code}(T_{t,(r,j)},f_{t,(r,j)})=(X_{t,(r,j)},E_{t,(r,j)},Y_{t,(r,j)})^{T}

with its own vertex vectors and edge vector as follows

X_{t,(r,j)}=(f_{t,(r,j)}(u_{t,(r,j),1})
,
f_{t,(r,j)}(u_{t,(r,j),2}),\dots,f_{t,(r,j)}(u_{t,(r,j),q(t,r,j)}))
,

E_{t,(r,j)}=(f_{t,(r,j)}(u_{t,(r,j),1}v_{t,(r,j),1}),f_{t,(r,j)}(u_{t,(r,j),2}v_{t,(r,j),2}),\dots,f_{t,(r,j)}(u_{t,(r,j),q(t,r,j)}v_{t,(r,j),q(t,r,j)}))
,

Y_{t,(r,j)}=(f_{t,(r,j)}(v_{t,(r,j),1}),f_{t,(r,j)}(v_{t,(r,j),2}),\dots,f_{t,(r,j)}(v_{t,(r,j),q(t,r,j)}))
.

We get a number-based string set

{\begin{split}U(T_{t,(r,j)})&=X_{t,(r,j)}\cup E_{t,(r,j)}\cup Y_{t,(r,j)}\\
&=\{f_{t,(r,j)}(z_{t,(r,j),1}),f_{t,(r,j)}(z_{t,(r,j),3}),\dots,f_{t,(r,j)}(z_{t,(r,j),3q(t,r,j)})\}\end{split}}

(131)

with
r\in[1,(3q_{t})!]
and
j\in[1,3q_{t}]
.

Each graph
F_{t}(w_{t,r,j})=T_{t,(r,j)}
distributes us a topological number-based string

f_{t,(r,j)}(z_{t,(r,j),1})f_{t,(r,j)}(z_{t,(r,j),3})\cdots f_{t,(r,j)}(z_{t,(r,j),3q(t,r,j)})=S^{q(t,r,j)}_{(r,j)}

then each graph-based string
S_{gb}(H_{t},r)
defined in Eq.(
130
) distributes us a
t
-rank topological number-based string

s(H_{t},r)=S^{q(t,r,1)q(t,r,2)\cdots q(t,r,3q_{t})}_{(r,1)(r,2)\cdots(r,3q_{t})}\leftarrow S_{gb}(H_{t},r)=F_{t}(w_{t,r,1})F_{t}(w_{t,r,2})\cdots F_{t}(w_{t,r,3q_{t}})

(132)

with
r\in[1,(3q_{t})!]
.

Step-II-
t
.3.
A
(p_{t+1},q_{t+1})
-graph
H_{t+1}
is obtained as: For each edge
xy\in E(H_{t})
, do a graph operation
O_{j}\in\textbf{{O}}
to the graphs
F_{t}(x)
and
F_{t}(xy)
for producing a subgraph
F_{t}(x)O_{j}F_{t}(xy)
, and do a graph operation
O_{j}\in\textbf{{O}}
to the graphs
F_{t}(y)
and
F_{t}(xy)
for producing a subgraph
F_{t}(y)O_{j}F_{t}(xy)
, then we get a subgraph (also graph-edge)
F_{t}(x)O_{j}F_{t}(xy)O_{j}F_{t}(y)
. Thereby, we obtain an
operation graphic lattice

\textbf{{L}}(\textbf{{Q}}_{t+1}[\textbf{{O}}]\textbf{{T}}_{t+1})=\big\{H[\textbf{{O}}]^{n_{t+1}}_{k=1}a_{t+1,k}T_{t+1,k}:~a_{t+1,k}\in Z^{0},~T_{t+1,k}\in\textbf{{T}}_{t+1},H\in\textbf{{Q}}_{t+1}\big\}

(133)

with
\sum^{n_{t+1}}_{k=1}a_{t+1,k}\geq 1
, and
\textbf{{Q}}_{s+1}=\textbf{{L}}(\textbf{{Q}}_{s}[\textbf{{O}}]\textbf{{T}}_{s})
for
s\geq 1
.

4.4
Complex number-based strings

By Eq.(
132
), we have a set of
t
-rank topological number-based strings as follows

T_{onbs}(H_{t})=\left\{s(H_{t},r)=S^{q(t,r,1)q(t,r,2)\cdots q(t,r,3q_{t})}_{(r,1)(r,2)\cdots(r,3q_{t})}:r\in[1,(3q_{t})!]\right\}

(134)

defined on the
(p_{t},q_{t})
-graph
H_{t}
admitting an edge-index total graph-coloring

F_{t}:V(H_{t})\cup E(H_{t})\rightarrow\textbf{{T}}_{t}

refer to Definition
31
. We have an operation

s(H_{t},i)[\oplus\ominus_{k}]~s(H_{t},j):=s(H_{t},i)[\oplus]s(H_{t},j)[\ominus]s(H_{t},k)=s(H_{t},\lambda)\in T_{onbs}(H_{t})

(135)

with the index
\lambda=i+j-k~(\bmod~M)
for a preappointed
zero

s(H_{t},k)\in T_{onbs}(H_{t})
. So, we get a
topological number-based string every-zero group

\{T_{onbs}(H_{t});\oplus\ominus\}
of order
M
.

Let
N_{1}=(3q_{t})!
, we have a
1
-rank complex topological number-based string

s^{1}_{per}(m)=s(H_{t},r_{1})s(H_{t},r_{2})\cdots s(H_{t},r_{N_{1}})

that is a permutation
s(H_{t},r_{1}),s(H_{t},r_{2}),\cdots,s(H_{t},r_{N_{1}})
of elements of the group
\{T_{onbs}(H_{t});\oplus\ominus\}
, so there are
(N_{1})!
permutations, we put them into a set
T^{1}_{onbs}(H_{t})=\{s^{1}_{per}(m):~m\in[1,(N_{1})!]\}
. Then we have an operation

s^{1}_{per}(i)[\oplus\ominus_{k}]~s^{1}_{per}(j):=s^{1}_{per}(i)[\oplus]s^{1}_{per}(j)[\ominus]s^{1}_{per}(k)=s^{1}_{per}(\lambda)\in T^{1}_{onbs}(H_{t})

(136)

with the index
\lambda=i+j-k~(\bmod~(N_{1})!)
for a preappointed
zero

s^{1}_{per}(k)\in T^{1}_{onbs}(H_{t})
, which has defined a
topological number-based string every-zero group

\{T^{1}_{onbs}(H_{t});\oplus\ominus\}
of order
(N_{1})!
.

Let
N_{2}=(N_{1})!
. The elements of the group
\left\{T^{1}_{onbs}(H_{t});\oplus\ominus\right\}
distributes us
(N_{2})!

2
-rank complex topological number-based strings of the form
s^{2}_{per}(m)=s^{1}_{per}(m_{1})s^{1}_{per}(m_{2})\cdots s^{1}_{per}(m_{N_{2}})
, we get a topological number-based string every-zero group
\{T^{2}_{onbs}(H_{t});\oplus\ominus\}
based on the number-based string set
T^{2}_{onbs}(H_{t})=\left\{s^{2}_{per}(m):~m\in[1,(N_{2})!]\right\}
. Go on in this way, we have topological number-based string every-zero groups
\left\{T^{k}_{onbs}(H_{t});\oplus\ominus\right\}
defined on the number-based string set

T^{k}_{onbs}(H_{t})=\left\{s^{k}_{per}(m):~m\in[1,(N_{k})!]\right\},~N_{k}=(N_{k-1})!,~k\geq 2

(137)

where the set
T^{k}_{onbs}(H_{t})
contains
k
-rank complex topological number-based strings
of order
N_{k}
.

The computational complexity of TOTAL-graph-coloring algorithm-II can be get from Eq.(
130
) and Eq.(
132
), we omit it.

5
Miscellaneous methods for generating strings

5.1
Number-based strings with parameters

Definition 32
.

[
34
]
A
parameterized Topcode-matrix

P_{(k,d)}=(X_{k,d},E_{k,d},Y_{k,d})^{T}
shown in Eq.(
138
) is defined as

X_{k,d}=(\alpha_{1}k+a_{1}d,\alpha_{2}k+a_{2}d,\dots,\alpha_{q}k+a_{q}d)
,
Y_{k,d}=(\beta_{1}k+b_{1}d,\beta_{2}k+b_{2}d,\dots,\beta_{q}k+b_{q}d)
, and

E_{k,d}=(\gamma_{1}k+c_{1}d,\gamma_{2}k+c_{2}d,\dots,\gamma_{q}k+c_{q}d)

for two parameters
k,d\geq 1
.
\square

Moreover we can partition a parameterized Topcode-matrix defined in Definition
32
into the linear combination of two Topcode-matrices as follows:

{\begin{split}P_{(k,d)}&=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\alpha_{1}k+a_{1}d&\alpha_{2}k+a_{2}d&\cdots&\alpha_{q}k+a_{q}d\\
\gamma_{1}k+c_{1}d&\gamma_{2}k+c_{2}d&\cdots&\gamma_{q}k+c_{q}d\\
\beta_{1}k+b_{1}d&\beta_{2}k+b_{2}d&\cdots&\beta_{q}k+b_{q}d\end{array}\right)_{3\times q}\\
&=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\alpha_{1}k&\alpha_{2}k&\cdots&\alpha_{q}k\\
\gamma_{1}k&\gamma_{2}k&\cdots&\gamma_{q}k\\
\beta_{1}k&\beta_{2}k&\cdots&\beta_{q}k\end{array}\right)_{3\times q}+\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}a_{1}d&a_{2}d&\cdots&a_{q}d\\
c_{1}d&c_{2}d&\cdots&c_{q}d\\
b_{1}d&b_{2}d&\cdots&b_{q}d\end{array}\right)_{3\times q}\\
&=k\cdot I_{3\times q}+d\cdot C_{3\times q}\end{split}}

(138)

where
I_{3\times q}
is called
constant Topcode-matrix
defined as

{\begin{split}I_{3\times q}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\alpha_{1}&\alpha_{2}&\cdots&\alpha_{q}\\
\gamma_{1}&\gamma_{2}&\cdots&\gamma_{q}\\
\beta_{1}&\beta_{2}&\cdots&\beta_{q}\end{array}\right)_{3\times q}=(I_{X},I_{E},I_{Y})^{T}\end{split}}

(139)

with three vectors
I_{X}=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\dots,\alpha_{q})
,
I_{E}=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\dots,\gamma_{q})
and
I_{Y}=(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\dots,\beta_{q})
; and
C_{3\times q}
, called
main Topcode-matrix
, is defined as

{\begin{split}C_{3\times q}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}a_{1}&a_{2}&\cdots&a_{q}\\
c_{1}&c_{2}&\cdots&c_{q}\\
b_{1}&b_{2}&\cdots&b_{q}\end{array}\right)_{3\times q}=(C_{X},C_{E},C_{Y})^{T}\end{split}}

(140)

for three vectors
C_{X}=(a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{q})
,
C_{E}=(c_{1},c_{2},\dots,c_{q})
, and
C_{Y}=(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{q})
. Also, the parameterized Topcode-matrix can be written as

{\begin{split}P_{(k,d)}=&k\cdot(I_{X},I_{E},I_{Y})^{T}+d\cdot(C_{X},C_{E},C_{Y})^{T}\\
=&(k\cdot I_{X}+d\cdot C_{X},k\cdot I_{E}+d\cdot C_{E},k\cdot I_{Y}+d\cdot C_{Y})^{T}\\
=&k\cdot I_{3\times q}+d\cdot C_{3\times q}.\end{split}}

(141)

Example 23
.

In Eq.(
138
), if we have an edge-difference constraint

(\gamma_{i}k+c_{i}d)+|(\beta_{i}k+b_{i}d)-(\alpha_{i}k+a_{i}d)|=(\gamma_{i}+\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i})k+(c_{i}+b_{i}-a_{i})d=k\cdot A_{ed}+d\cdot B_{ed}

for
i\in[1,q]
, we write the above fact as
I_{E}+|I_{Y}-I_{X}|:=A_{ed}
and
C_{E}+|C_{Y}-C_{X}|:=B_{ed}
, where
A_{ed}
and
B_{ed}
are constants.

If there is an edge-magic constraint

(\gamma_{i}k+c_{i}d)+(\beta_{i}k+b_{i}d)+(\alpha_{i}k+a_{i}d=(\gamma_{i}+\beta_{i}+\alpha_{i})k+(c_{i}+b_{i}+a_{i})d)=k\cdot A_{em}+d\cdot B_{em}

for
i\in[1,q]
, we write the above fact as
I_{X}+I_{E}+I_{Y}:=A_{em}
and
C_{X}+C_{E}+C_{Y}:=B_{em}
for constants
A_{em}
and
B_{em}
.

If we have a graceful-difference constraint

\big||(\beta_{i}k+b_{i}d)-(\alpha_{i}k+a_{i}d)|-(\gamma_{i}k+c_{i}d)\big|=(\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i}-\gamma_{i})k+(b_{i}-a_{i}-c_{i})d=k\cdot A_{gd}+d\cdot B_{gd}

for
i\in[1,q]
, we write the above fact as
\big||I_{Y}-I_{X}|-I_{E}\big|:=A_{gd}
and
\big||C_{Y}-C_{X}|-C_{E}\big|:=B_{gd}
for constants
A_{gd}
and
B_{gd}
.

If we have felicitous-difference constraint

|(\beta_{i}k+b_{i}d)+(\alpha_{i}k+a_{i}d)-(\gamma_{i}k+c_{i}d)|=(\beta_{i}+\alpha_{i}-\gamma_{i})k+(b_{i}+a_{i}-c_{i})d=k\cdot A_{fd}+d\cdot B_{fd}

for
i\in[1,q]
, we write the above fact as
|I_{Y}+I_{X}-I_{E}|:=A_{fd}
and
|C_{Y}+C_{X}-C_{E}|:=B_{fd}
for constants
A_{fd}
and
B_{fd}
.
\square

If there is no confusion, we omit “order
3\times q
” in the following discussion, or add a sentence “ the Topcode-matrices
I
,
T_{code}(G)
and
P_{(k,d)}(G)
have the same order”. For
bipartite graphs
, especially, we define the
unite Topcode-matrix
as follows

{\begin{split}I\,^{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}0&0&\cdots&0\\
1&1&\cdots&1\\
1&1&\cdots&1\end{array}\right)_{3\times q}=(X\,^{0},~E\,^{0},~Y\,^{0})^{T}\end{split}}

(142)

with two vertex-vectors
X\,^{0}=(0,0,\dots,0)_{1\times q}
and
Y\,^{0}=(1,1,\dots,1)_{1\times q}
, and the edge-vector
E\,^{0}=(1,1,\dots,1)_{1\times q}
.

Definition 33
.

[
34
]
Let
G
be a bipartite
(p,q)
-graph with
V(G)=X\cup Y
and
X\cap Y=\emptyset
, and let
k,d
be integers. If
G
admits a set-ordered
W
-constraint coloring
f
, that is
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
, so we get a
parameterized Topcode-matrix
defined by

{\begin{split}P_{(k,d)}(G,F)&=k\cdot I\,^{0}+d\cdot T_{code}(G,f)\\
&=\left(\begin{array}[]{rrrrr}f(u_{1})d&f(u_{2})d&\cdots&f(u_{q})d\\
k+f(u_{1}v_{1})d&k+f(u_{2}v_{2})d&\cdots&k+f(u_{q}v_{q})d\\
k+f(v_{1})d&k+f(v_{2})d&\cdots&k+f(v_{q})d\end{array}\right)\end{split}}

(143)

where three Topcode-matrices
I\,^{0}
,
T_{code}(G,f)
and
P_{(k,d)}(G,F)
have the same order
3\times q
, and
F
is a
W
-constraint parameterized coloring
of
G
, as well as

\centering{\begin{split}T_{code}(G,f)=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}f(u_{1})&f(u_{2})&\cdots&f(u_{q})\\
f(u_{1}v_{1})&f(u_{2}v_{2})&\cdots&f(u_{q}v_{q})\\
f(v_{1})&f(v_{2})&\cdots&f(v_{q})\end{array}\right)\end{split}}\@add@centering

(144)

hoding the
W
-constraint
f(u_{k}v_{k})=W\langle f(u_{k}),f(v_{k})\rangle
for each edge
u_{k}v_{k}\in E(G)
with
u_{k}\in X
and
v_{k}\in Y
.
\square

Remark 19
.

The assignment Topcode-matrices.
By Definition
11
, we have a
assignment Topcode-matrix

T_{code}(G,f):=P_{(k,d)}(G,F)
defined in Eq.(
143
), which converts a parameterized number-based string

s(k,d)=c_{1}(k,d)c_{2}(k,d)\cdots c_{3q}(k,d)

(145)

with longer bytes made by
P_{(k,d)}(G,F)
defined in Definition
33
to a string
s=c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{3q}
with shorter bytes made by
T_{code}(G,f)
.

The fractional strings.
The parameterized Topcode-matrix
P_{(k,d)}(G,F)
is useful in the discussion of fractional strings. The limitation
(k,d)\rightarrow(k_{0},d_{0})
enables us to induce real-valued strings. For example, a
fractional
(k_{n},d_{n})
-string

s^{*}_{n}=c^{*}_{n,1}c^{*}_{n,2}\cdots c^{*}_{n,m}
holds:

(i) there is at least one
c^{*}_{n,j}
to be a positive fractional number;

(ii)
(k_{n},d_{n})\rightarrow(k_{0},d_{0})
as
n\rightarrow\infty
;

(iii) there is a positive integer
M_{n}
for each
n
holding

M_{n}[\bullet]s^{*}_{n}=(M_{n}\cdot c^{*}_{n,1})(M_{n}\cdot c^{*}_{n,2})\cdots(M_{n}\cdot c^{*}_{n,m})=s_{n}

such that
s_{n}
is just a
proper number-based string
with positive integer
M_{n}\cdot c^{*}_{n,j}
for
j\in[1,m]
. In other words, the research of fractional strings can be translated into the investigation of proper number-based strings.
\parallel

Definition 34
.

∗
We call a parameterized number-based string
s(k,d)
made by the parameterized Topcode-matrix
P_{(k,d)}(G,F)
defined in Eq.(
143
)
plane-curve-attached string
. Let
pc(x,y)=0
be a
plane curve
defined on a domain
[\alpha,\beta]^{r}
for
0\leq\alpha<\beta
. If there are positive integer points
(k_{n},d_{n})\in[\alpha,\beta]^{r}
holding
pc(k_{n},d_{n})=0
for integers
k_{n},d_{n}\geq 0
with
n\in[1,m]
, then we get a
plane-curve-attached string sequence

\{s(k_{n},d_{n})\}^{m}_{n=1}
based on the plane curve
pc(x,y)=0
.
\square

Theorem 33
.

∗
By Definition
34
, there are infinite plane-curve-attached string sequences based on a parameterized Topcode-matrix
P_{(k,d)}(G,F)
defined in Eq.(
143
) and infinite plane curves, which provides the theoretical basis for the one-encryption one-time.

Remark 20
.

For a
public-key graph

G
admitting a
W
-constraint parameterized coloring
F
, we use this
parameter-colored graph

G
and a plane curve
pc(x,y)=0
to form a
private-key graph
in a
topological signature authentication
, the private-key graph is denoted as
H=\langle G,F,pc(x,y)=0\rangle
. Since there are infinite real-valued functions and there are infinite integer points in a plane curve, so we can get infinite number-based strings to encrypt or to decrypt a file consisted of many segments in the method of
asymmetric topology cryptography
, and these number-based strings are random since the plane curve are taken randomly in the private-key graphs like as
H=\langle G,F,pc(x,y)=0\rangle
.

If the plane curve
pc(x,y)=0
is an elliptic curve:
y^{2}=x^{3}+ax^{2}+bx+c
defined on a finite field
[0,A_{prime}]
with a prime number
A_{prime}
, then deciphering the plane-curve-attached string sequence
\{s(k_{n},d_{n})\}^{m}_{n=1}
is even more difficult, even impossible.
\parallel

5.2
(abc)
-linear colorings

Theorem 34
.

[
34
]
If a bipartite and connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a
set-ordered

W
-constraint total coloring
f
defined in Definition
2
, Definition
3
and Definition
4
. For positive integers
a,b,c
and non-negative integer
\lambda
, if there are

(i) the
(abc)
-edge-magic constraint
af(x)+bf(y)+cf(xy)=\lambda
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
; or

(ii) the
(abc)
-felicitous-difference constraint
|af(x)+bf(y)-cf(xy)|=\lambda
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
; or

(iii) the
(abc)
-graceful-difference constraint
\big||af(x)-bf(y)|-cf(xy)\big|=\lambda
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
; or

(iv) the
(abc)
-edge-difference constraint
cf(xy)+|af(x)-bf(y)|=c
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.

Then this bipartite and connected graph
G
admits a
W
-constraint
(k,d)
-total coloring
F
holding each one of the above four
(abc)
-constraints.

Proof.

Let
(X,Y)
be the vertex bipartition of a bipartite
(p,q)
-graph
G
, so a set-ordered
W
-constraint total coloring
f
of
G
holds
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
true. There is a
W
-constraint
(k,d)
-total coloring
F
defined by setting
F(x)=f(x)d
,
F(y)=k+f(y)d
and
F(xy)=k+f(xy)d
made by the parameterized Topcode-matrix
P_{(k,d)}(G,F)
defined in Eq.(
143
), so we have:

(i) If the
(abc)
-edge-magic constraint
af(x)+bf(y)+cf(xy)=\lambda
holds true for each edge
xy\in E(G)
, then the
(abc)
-edge-magic constraint

{\begin{split}aF(x)+bF(y)+cF(xy)&=af(x)d+b[k+f(y)d]+c[k+f(xy)d]\\
&=[af(x)+bf(y)+cf(xy)]d+(b+c)k\\
&=d\cdot\lambda+(b+c)k\end{split}}

(146)

holds true for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.

(ii) If the
(abc)
-felicitous-difference constraint
|af(x)+bf(y)-cf(xy)|=\lambda
holds true for each edge
xy\in E(G)
, then we get the
(abc)
-felicitous-difference constraint

{\begin{split}\big|aF(x)+bF(y)-cF(xy)\big|&=\big|af(x)d+b[k+f(y)d]-c[k+f(xy)d]\big|\\
&=\big|[af(x)+bf(y)-cf(xy)]d+(b-c)k\big|\\
&\leq\big|af(x)+bf(y)-cf(xy)\big|d+\big|b-c\big|k\\
&=d\cdot\lambda+|b-c|k\end{split}}

(147)

for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.

(iii) If the
(abc)
-graceful-difference constraint
\big||af(x)-bf(y)|-cf(xy)\big|=\lambda
holds true for each edge
xy\in E(G)
, thus, the
(abc)
-graceful-difference constraint

{\begin{split}\big||aF(x)-bF(y)|-cF(xy)\big|&=\big||af(x)d-b[k+f(y)d]|-c[k+f(xy)d]\big|\\
&\leq\big|bf(y)-af(x)-cf(xy)\big|d+|b-c|k\\
&=d\cdot\lambda+|b-c|k\end{split}}

(148)

or

{\begin{split}\big||aF(x)-bF(y)|-cF(xy)\big|&=\big||af(x)d-b[k+f(y)d]|-c[k+f(xy)d]\big|\\
&\leq\big|af(x)-bf(y)-cf(xy)\big|d+|b-c|k\\
&=d\cdot\lambda+|b-c|k\end{split}}

(149)

holds true for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.

(iv) If the
(abc)
-edge-difference constraint
cf(xy)+|af(x)-bf(y)|=\lambda
holds true for each edge
xy\in E(G)
, then we have the
(abc)
-graceful-difference constraint

{\begin{split}cF(xy)+\big|aF(x)-bF(y)\big|&=c[k+f(xy)d]+|af(x)d-b[k+f(y)d]|\\
&\leq[cf(xy)+|af(x)-bf(y)|]d+(b+c)k\\
&=d\cdot\lambda+(b+c)k\end{split}}

(150)

for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.

Moreover, we have other two relations as follows:

{\begin{split}|aF(x)-bF(y)|&=|b[k+f(y)d]-af(x)d|\leq|bf(y)-af(x)|d+bk\\
&=c[k+f(xy)d]+(b-c)k\\
&=cF(xy)+(b-c)k\end{split}}

if
cf(xy)=|bf(y)-af(x)|
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
; and moreover

{\begin{split}aF(x)+bF(y)&=af(x)d+b[k+f(y)d]=[bf(y)+af(x)]d+bk\\
&=c[k+f(xy)d]+(b-c)k\\
&=cF(xy)+(b-c)k\end{split}}

if
cf(xy)=af(x)+bf(y)
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.

The proof of the theorem is complete.
∎

Remark 21
.

About Theorem
34
, trees admit some
set-ordered

W
-constraint total colorings defined in Definition
2
, Definition
3
and Definition
4
; refer to Theorem
8
. In Theorem
34
, the parameterized Topcode-matrix
P_{(a,b,c)}(G,F)
of the bipartite and connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admitting the
W
-constraint
(k,d)
-total coloring
F
holding each one of four
(abc)
-constraints can provide us more complex number-based strings for real application.

The ABC-conjecture (also, Oesterlé-Masser conjecture, 1985) involves the equation
a+b=c
and the relationship between prime numbers. Proving or disproving the ABC-conjecture could impact: Diophantine (polynomial) math problems including Tijdeman’s theorem, Vojta’s conjecture, Erdös-Woods conjecture, Fermat’s last theorem, Wieferich prime and Roth’s theorem, and so on
[
26
]
.
\parallel

Definition 35
.

∗
Suppose that
f
is a
W
-constraint total coloring of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, so we have its own Topcode-matrix
T_{code}(G,f)
. We define new colorings by the
W
-constraint total coloring
f
as follows:

Pacolo
-1.

Setting a new total coloring
\alpha(w)=f(w)+x+y+z
for each element
w\in V(G)\cup E(G)
, where
(x,y,z)
is an integer point of a space curve
F(x,y,z)=0
with integers
x,y,z\geq 0
.

Pacolo
-2.

Letting a new total coloring
\beta(w)=s(x,y,z)f(w)+\lambda
for each element
w\in V(G)\cup E(G)
, where

s(x,y,z)=c_{1}(x,y,z)c_{2}(x,y,z)\cdots c_{m}(x,y,z)

(151)

is a parameterized number-based string, and
\lambda=a_{1}a_{2}\cdots a_{n}
is a number-based string, and
(x,y,z)
is an integer point of a space curve
F(x,y,z)=0
with integers
x,y,z\geq 0
.

Pacolo
-3.

Defining a new total coloring
\gamma(w)=s(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})f(w)+\mu
for
w\in V(G)\cup E(G)
, where

s(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})=c_{1}(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})c_{2}(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})\cdots c_{m}(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})

(152)

is a parameterized number-based string, and
\mu=b_{1}b_{2}\cdots b_{s}
is a number-based string, as well as
(x_{1},x_{2}
,
\dots
,
x_{n})
is an integer point of a
n
-dimension curve
F(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})=0
with integers
x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n}\geq 0
.

Since the total colorings
\alpha,\beta
and
\gamma
defined above are based one curves
F(x,y,z)=0
and
F(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})=0
, as well as the
W
-constraint total coloring
f
of the
(p,q)
-graph
G
, we call them
n
-dimension curve-attached
W
-constraint total colorings
, and the number-based strings induced by the parameterized
W
-constraint Topcode-matrices
T_{code}(G,\alpha)
,
T_{code}(G,\beta)
and
T_{code}(G,\gamma)
are called
n
-dimension curve-attached
W
-constraint number-based strings
.
\square

Example 24
.

As the application of Theorem
34
, we let
(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})
be an integer point of a space curve
F(x,y,z)=0
with non-negative integers
x_{k},y_{k},z_{k}\geq 0
for
k\in[1,M]
. By Definition
35
, we get a
3
-dimension curve-attached
W
-constraint total colorings
\beta_{k}(w)=s(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})f(w)+\lambda_{k}
for each element
w\in V(G)\cup E(G)
with

s(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})=c_{1}(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})c_{2}(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})\cdots c_{m}(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})

(153)

and
\lambda_{k}=a_{k,1}a_{k,2}\cdots a_{k,n_{k}}
for
k\in[1,M]
.

The parameterized Topcode-matrix
T_{code}(G,\beta_{k})
with
k\in[1,M]
induces
M\cdot(3q)!
number-based strings of form
\beta_{k}(w_{j_{1}})\beta_{k}(w_{j_{2}})\cdots\beta_{k}(w_{j_{3q}})
, where
\beta_{k}(w_{j_{1}}),\beta_{k}(w_{j_{2}}),\dots,\beta_{k}(w_{j_{3q}})
is a permutation of the elements of the parameterized Topcode-matrix
T_{code}(G,\beta_{k})
.

Then we have:

(i) The edge-magic constraint

{\begin{split}\beta_{k}(u)+\beta_{k}(uv)+\beta_{k}(v)=&s(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})\big[f(u)+f(uv)+f(v)\big]+3\lambda_{k}\\
=&s(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})\mu_{1}+3\lambda_{k}\end{split}}

(154)

if the edge-magic constraint
f(u)+f(uv)+f(v)=\mu_{1}
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds true.

(ii) The edge-difference constraint

{\begin{split}\beta_{k}(uv)+|\beta_{k}(u)-\beta_{k}(v)|=&s(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})\big[f(uv)+|f(u)-f(v)|\big]+\lambda_{k}\\
=&s(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})\mu_{2}+\lambda_{k}\end{split}}

(155)

if the edge-difference constraint
f(uv)+|f(u)-f(v)|=\mu_{2}
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds true.

(iii) The graceful-difference constraint

{\begin{split}\big||\beta_{k}(u)-\beta_{k}(v)|-\beta_{k}(uv)\big|\leq&s(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})\big||f(u)-f(v)|-f(uv)\big|+\lambda_{k}\\
=&s(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})\mu_{3}+\lambda_{k}\end{split}}

(156)

if the graceful-difference constraint
\big||f(u)-f(v)|-f(uv)\big|=\mu_{3}
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds true.

(iv) The felicitous-difference constraint

{\begin{split}|\beta_{k}(u)+\beta_{k}(v)-\beta_{k}(uv)|\leq&s(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})\big|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)\big|+\lambda_{k}\\
=&s(x_{k},y_{k},z_{k})\mu_{4}+\lambda_{k}\end{split}}

(157)

if the felicitous-difference constraint
\big|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)\big|=\mu_{4}
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds true.
\square

Theorem 35
.

Let
f
be a
W
-constraint total coloring of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, and
F(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})=0
be a
n
-dimension curve. Then there are infinite
n
-dimension curve-attached
W
-constraint total colorings

\gamma(w)=s(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})f(w)+\mu,~w\in V(G)\cup E(G)

defined in Definition
35
, where each
x_{i}
in the string
s(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})
for an integer point
(x_{1},x_{2},\dots
,
x_{n})
of the

.

5.3
Set-based strings

Definition 36
.

[
52
]
Let a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admit a mapping
F:X\rightarrow S
, where
S
is a subset of subsets of the power set
[0,pq]^{2}
, and let
Rs(m)=\{c_{1},c_{2},\dots,c_{m}\}
be a given constraint set. There are the following constraints:

(a)

X=V(G)
.

(b)

X=E(G)
.

(c)

X=V(G)\cup E(G)
.

(d)

F(u)\not=F(v)
if
uv\in E(G)
(it may happen
F(u)\cap F(v)\neq\emptyset
).

(e)

F(uv)\not=F(uw)
for each pair of adjacent edges
uv
and
uw
of
G
.

(f)

F(x)\not=F(y)
for distinct vertices
x
and
y
of
G
.

(g)

F(xy)\not=F(uv)
for different edges
uv
and
xy
of
G
.

(h)

A mapping
F\,^{\prime}:E(G)\rightarrow S
is induced by
F
subject to
Rs(m)
, that is, each edge
uv\in E(G)
is labelled by a number of the set
F\,^{\prime}(uv)
such that each
c\in F\,^{\prime}(uv)
is generated by some
a\in F(u)
,
b\in F(v)
and one constraint or more constraints of
Rs(m)
.

Then

(1-1)
F
is called a
vertex-distinguishing set-labeling
of
G
if the constraints (
a
) and (
f
) hold true.

(1-2)
F
is called an
edge-distinguishing set-labeling
of
G
if the constraints (
b
) and (
g
) hold true.

(1-3)
F
is called a
all-distinguishing set-coloring
of
G
if (
c
), (
f
) and (
g
) hold true.

(1-4)
F\,^{\prime}
is called an
induced edge-distinguishing edge set-labeling
of
G
if both constraints (
g
) and (
h
) hold true.

(1-5)
(F,F\,^{\prime})
is called an
induced all-distinguishing set-coloring
subject to
Rs(m)
if the constraints (
a
), (
f
), (
g
) and (
h
) hold true.

(2-1)
F
is called a
proper set-labeling
of
G
if it satisfies the constraints (
a
) and (
d
) simultaneously.

(2-2)
F
is called a
proper edge set-labeling
of
G
if it satisfies the constraints (
b
) and (
e
) simultaneously.

(2-3)
F
is called a
proper total set-coloring
of
G
if it satisfies the constraints (
c
), (
d
) and (
e
) simultaneously.

(2-4)
(F,F\,^{\prime})
is called an
induced proper total set-coloring
subject to
Rs(m)
if the constraints (
a
), (
d
), (
e
) and (
h
) hold true simultaneously.

(3-1)
F
is called a
pseudo-vertex set-labeling
of
G
if it holds the constraints (
a
), but not (
d
).

(3-2)
F
is called a
pseudo-edge set-labeling
of
G
if it holds the constraints (
b
), but not (
e
).

(3-3)
F
is called a
pseudo-total set-coloring
of
G
if it holds the constraint (
c
), but not (
d
), or not (
e
), or not both (
d
) and (
e
).
\square

Definition 37
.

[
52
]
We have the following set-labelings/set-colorings:

(i) There are two parameters

v_{s}=\min\{|F(x)|:x\in V(G)\},~v_{l}=\max\{|F(y)|:y\in V(G)\}

by Definition
36
. Then the set-labeling/set-coloring
F
is called an
\alpha
-uniformly vertex set-labeling/set-coloring
of
G
if
v_{s}=v_{l}=\alpha
. As
\alpha=1
,
(F,F\,^{\prime})
is a popular labeling of graph theory.

(ii) There are two parameters

e_{s}=\min\{|F\,^{\prime}(uv)|:~uv\in E(G)\},~e_{l}=\max\{|F\,^{\prime}(xy)|:~xy\in E(G)\}

from Definition
36
. Then the edge set-labeling/set-coloring
F\,^{\prime}
is called a
\beta
-uniformly edge set-labeling/set-coloring
of
G
if
e_{s}=e_{l}=\beta
.

(iii) Definition
36
induces two parameters

t_{s}=\min\{|F(x)|:x\in V(G)\cup E(G)\},~t_{l}=\max\{|F(y)|:~y\in V(G)\cup E(G)\}

If
k=t_{s}=t_{l}
, then the total set-labeling/set-coloring
F
is called
k
-uniformly total set-labeling/set-coloring
.
\square

Remark 22
.

The colorings defined in Definition
36
and Definition
37
form set-type Topcode-matrices, which can induce more complex number-based strings.
\parallel

5.3.1
Distinguishing set-colorings

Definition 38
.

∗
Let
H
be a
(p,q)
-graph, and let
Q
be a set of subsets with each subset
e\in Q
holding
e\neq\emptyset
true. There is a
set-coloring

\theta:S\rightarrow Q
for
S\subseteq V(H)\cup E(H)
. There are the following constraints:

Dint
-1.

S=V(H)
.

Dint
-2.

S=E(H)
.

Dint
-3.

S=V(H)\cup E(H)
.

—
local distinguishing

Dint
-4.

\theta(u)\neq\theta(v)
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
.

Dint
-5.

\theta(uv)\neq\theta(uw)
for
v,w\in N_{ei}(u)
and each vertex
u\in V(H)
.

Dint
-6.

F(u)\neq F(uv)
and
F(v)\neq F(uv)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
.

—
local intersected

Dint
-7.

\theta(u)\cap\theta(v)\neq\emptyset
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
.

Dint
-8.

\theta(u)\cap\theta(v)\subseteq\theta(uv)
and
|\theta(u)\cap\theta(v)|\geq r\geq 1
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
.

Dint
-9.

\theta(uv)\cap\theta(u)\neq\emptyset
and
\theta(uv)\cap\theta(v)\neq\emptyset
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
.

Dint
-10.

\theta(uv)\cap\theta(uw)\neq\emptyset
for
v,w\in N_{ei}(u)
and each vertex
u\in V(H)
.

—
v-adjacent distinguishing

Dint
-11.

\bigcup_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}\theta(v)\neq\bigcup_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}\theta(z)
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

Dint
-12.

\bigcap_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}\theta(v)\neq\bigcap_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}\theta(z)
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

Dint
-13.

\theta(u)\cup\big[\bigcup_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}\theta(v)\big]\neq\theta(w)\cup\big[\bigcup_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}\theta(z)\big]
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

Dint
-14.

\theta(u)\cap\big[\bigcap_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}\theta(v)\big]\neq\theta(w)\cap\big[\bigcap_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}\theta(z)\big]
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

—
e-adjacent distinguishing

Dint
-15.

\bigcup_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}\theta(uv)\neq\bigcup_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}\theta(wz)
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

Dint
-16.

\bigcap_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}\theta(uv)\neq\bigcap_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}\theta(wz)
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

—
ve-adjacent distinguishing

Dint
-17.

\theta(u)\cup\big[\bigcup_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}\theta(uv)\big]\neq\theta(w)\cup\big[\bigcup_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}\theta(wz)\big]
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

Dint
-18.

\theta(u)\cap\big[\bigcap_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}\theta(uv)\big]\neq\theta(w)\cap\big[\bigcap_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}\theta(wz)\big]
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

We call
\theta

5.3.2
Hyperedge-set colorings

Definition 39
.

∗
Let
G
be a
(p,q)
-graph, and let
\Lambda
be a finite set of numbers. There is a
hyperedge-set coloring

F:S\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
, where
\mathcal{E}
is a
hyperedge set
of the power set
\Lambda^{2}
holding
\Lambda=\bigcup_{e\in\mathcal{E}}e
, and
S\subseteq V(G)\cup E(G)
. There are the following constraints:

Hyset
-1.

S=V(G)
.

Hyset
-2.

S=E(G)
.

Hyset
-3.

S=V(G)\cup E(G)
.

—
local distinguishing

Hyset
-4.

F(u)\neq F(v)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
.

Hyset
-5.

F(uv)\neq F(uw)
for adjacent edges
uv,uw\in E(G)
and
u\in V(G)
.

Hyset
-6.

F(u)\neq F(uv)
and
F(v)\neq F(uv)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
.

—
local intersected

Hyset
-7.

F(u)\cap F(v)\neq\emptyset
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
.

Hyset
-8.

F(uv)\cap F(uw)\neq\emptyset
for adjacent edges
uv,uw\in E(G)
.

Hyset
-9.

F(u)\cap F(v)\subseteq F(uv)
and
F(u)\cap F(v)\neq\emptyset
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
.

Hyset
-10.

F(uv)\cap F(u)\neq\emptyset
and
F(uv)\cap F(v)\neq\emptyset
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
.

—
v-adjacent distinguishing

Hyset
-11.

\bigcup_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}F(v)\neq\bigcup_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}F(z)
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

Hyset
-12.

\bigcap_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}F(v)\neq\bigcap_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}F(z)
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

Hyset
-13.

F(u)\cup\big[\bigcup_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}F(v)\big]\neq F(w)\cup\big[\bigcup_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}F(z)\big]
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

Hyset
-14.

F(u)\cap\big[\bigcap_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}F(v)\big]\neq F(w)\cap\big[\bigcap_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}F(z)\big]
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

—
e-adjacent distinguishing

Hyset
-15.

\bigcup_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}F(uv)\neq\bigcup_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}F(wz)
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

Hyset
-16.

\bigcap_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}F(uv)\neq\bigcap_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}F(wz)
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

—
ve-adjacent distinguishing

Hyset
-17.

F(u)\cup\big[\bigcup_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}F(uv)\big]\neq F(w)\cup\big[\bigcup_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}F(wz)\big]
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

Hyset
-18.

F(u)\cap\big[\bigcap_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}F(uv)\big]\neq F(w)\cap\big[\bigcap_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}F(wz)\big]
for each edge
uw\in V(H)
.

Then

—
