Definition 40
.

∗
For Definition
39
, we add a
Feedback-constraint
: Each pair of hyperedges
e,e\,^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}
holding
|e\cap e\,^{\prime}|\geq 1
corresponds to an edge
xy\in E(G)
, such that
F(x)=e
and
F(y)=e\,^{\prime}
.

Suppose that a graph
G
admits a
W
-constraint hyperedge-set coloring defined in Definition
39
. If the graph
G
satisfies the Feedback-constraint, then we call
G
the
W
-constraint graph
of the hypergraph
\mathcal{H}_{yper}=(\Lambda,\mathcal{E})
.
\square

Definition 41
.

Let
\Lambda
be a set of finite elements. A graph
G
has its own vertex set
V(G)=\mathcal{E}
, where
\mathcal{E}
is a
hyperedge set
holding
\Lambda=\bigcup_{e\in\mathcal{E}}e
, and each edge
e_{x}e_{y}\in E(G)
means
e_{x}\cap e_{y}\neq\emptyset
, then we call
G

intersected graph
of the hypergraph
\mathcal{H}_{yper}=(\Lambda,\mathcal{E})
.

Remark 23
.

Definition
40
enables us to know more complex hypergraphs through
W
-constraint graphs, for example, a hypergraph
\mathcal{H}_{yper}=(\Lambda,\mathcal{E})
with its own
ee
-
[ve]
-intersected adjacent-
v
distinguishing hyperedge set
\mathcal{E}
defined in Definition
39
.

Clearly, some topological structures of the intersected graph
G
are just what the hypergraph
\mathcal{H}_{yper}=(\Lambda,\mathcal{E})
has by Definition
41
, so a hypergraph is equivalent to its intersected graph. Thereby, intersected graphs help us to know hypergraphs, and moreover some properties of the intersected graphs are just what corresponding hypergraphs have; refer to
[
33
]
.
\parallel

Remark 24
.

The edge-set-coloring 1-2-3-conjecture.
If there is an
edge set-coloring

\theta:E(H)\rightarrow Q
for a connected graph
H
, such that the vertex induced color holding

\sum_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}|\theta(uv)|=:\theta(u)\neq\theta(w):=\sum_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}|\theta(wz)|

(158)

for each edge
uw\in V(H)
, then we call
\theta
a
proper vertex set-coloring
of the connected graph
H
, where
Q
is a set of subsets with different cardinalities, or
Q
is a hyperedge set. Motivated from the 1-2-3-conjecture, we propose the
edge-set-coloring 1-2-3-conjecture
:
The cardinality
|Q|\leq 3
is enough for every connected graph
H
to admits an edge set-coloring
\theta
based on
Q
such that
\theta
induces a proper vertex set-coloring
of
H
.
\parallel

Theorem 36
.

∗
If the intersected graph
G
of a hypergraph
\mathcal{H}_{yper}=(\Lambda,\mathcal{E})
supports the
1-2-3-conjecture
, then
\mathcal{H}_{yper}=(\Lambda,\mathcal{E})
supports the
edge-set-coloring 1-2-3-conjecture
too.

Remark 25
.

For vector
\overrightarrow{d}_{i}=(a_{i},b_{i},c_{i})
, the
edge-vector-coloring 1-2-3-conjecture
: Each connected graph
G
admits an
edge vector-coloring

f:E(G)\rightarrow V_{ector}=\{\overrightarrow{d}_{i}=(a_{i},b_{i},c_{i}):i\in[1,3]\}
such that
f
colors properly the vertices of
G
, that is

\sum_{v\in N_{ei}(u)}\|f(uv)\|=:f(u)\neq f(w):=\sum_{z\in N_{ei}(w)}\|f(wz)\|

(159)

for each edge
uw\in E(G)
.
\parallel

5.4
Integer partition into magic-constraints

Since number-based strings were induced from the colorings of graphs, we present some theorems for determining graphs admitting colorings with the magic-constraints in this subsection.

Theorem 37
.

The set
S_{ema}(m)
contains each group of mutually distinct positive integers
a,b,c
holding the
ema
-constraint
m=a+b+c
for a fixed integer
m\geq 5
. There is at least a connected graph
G
admitting a proper total coloring
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow S_{ema}(m)
, such that each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds the edge-magic constraint
f(u)+f(v)+f(uv)=m
with
f(u)=a
,
f(v)=b
and
f(uv)=c
, and
\Delta(G)\leq m-5
, or
\Delta(G)\leq m-4
, and
G
contains a path
P
of length being greater than any given positive integer, as well as the edge number
|E(G)|
can be as big as it wants.

Proof.

G=K_{1,m-4}
,
V(K_{1,m-4})=\{u,v_{i}:i\in[1,m-4]\}
,
E(K_{1,m-4})=\{uv_{i}:i\in[1,m-4]\}
;
f(u)=1
,
f(uv_{i})=i+1
and
f(v_{i})=m-2-i
for
i\in[1,m-4]
. Clearly,
f(u)+f(uv_{i})+f(v_{i})=m
and
\Delta(K_{1,m-4})\leq m-4
; see Fig.
19
(a).

If
i+1=m-2-i
, or
2i=m-3
, we get another connected graph
K_{1,m-4}-uv_{i}+v_{1}v_{i}
, and add
f(v_{1}v_{i})=i
, here
\Delta(K_{1,m-4}-uv_{i}+v_{1}v_{i})\leq m-5
; see Fig.
19
(b).

We omit the proofs about the path length and the edge number, since they can be obtained by the polynomial construction methods; see Fig.
19
(c).
∎

Figure 19:
Examples for the integer partition under the edge-magic constraint.

Theorem 38
.

The set
S_{edi}(m)
for a fixed integer
m\geq 5
contains each group of mutually distinct positive integers
a,b,c
holding the
edi
-constraint
m=edi\langle a,b,c\rangle=c+|a-b|
. For given integers
d\geq 2
and
r\geq 2
, there is a connected graph
H
admitting a proper total coloring
g:V(H)\cup E(H)\rightarrow S_{edi}(m)
, such that
g(u)=a
,
g(v)=b
and
g(uv)=c
, and

(i) each edge
uv\in E(H)
holds the edge-difference constraint
g(uv)+|g(u)-g(v)|=m
true;

(ii) the maximal degree
\Delta(H)<m
;

(iii) the edge number
|E(H)|\geq r
;

(iii) the graph
H
contains a path
P
of length no less than
d
.

Figure 20:
Examples for illustrating Theorem
38
under the edge-difference constraint.

Theorem 39
.

The set
S_{fdi}(m)
for a fixed integer
m\geq 5
contains each group of mutually distinct positive integers
a,b,c
holding the
fdi
-constraint
m=fdi\langle a,b,c\rangle=|a+b-c|
. Then there are infinite elements in
S_{fdi}(m)
, and for given integers
\Delta\geq 2
,
d\geq 2
and
r\geq 2
, there is a connected graph
T
admitting a proper total coloring
h:V(T)\cup E(T)\rightarrow S_{fdi}(m)
, such that
h(u)=a
,
h(v)=b
and
h(uv)=c
, and

(i) the felicitous-difference constraint
|h(u)+h(v)-h(uv)|=m
for each edge
uv\in E(T)
holds true;

(ii) the maximal degree
\Delta(T)=\Delta
;

(iii) the edge number
|E(T)|\geq r
;

(iii) the graph
T
contains a path
P
of length no less than
d
.

Figure 21:
A diagram for illustrating Theorem
39
under the felicitous-difference constraint.

Theorem 40
.

The set
S_{gdi}(m)
for a fixed integer
m\geq 0
contains each group of mutually distinct positive integers
a,b,c
holding the
gdi
-constraint
m=gdi\langle a,b,c\rangle=\big||a-b|-c\big|
. Then there are infinite elements in
S_{gdi}(m)
, and for given integers
\Delta\geq 2
,
d\geq 2
and
r\geq 2
, there is a connected graph
J
admitting a proper total coloring
\theta:V(J)\cup E(J)\rightarrow S_{gdi}(m)
, such that
\theta(u)=a
,
\theta(v)=b
and
\theta(uv)=c
, and

(i) the graceful-difference constraint
\big||\theta(u)-\theta(v)|-\theta(uv)\big|=m
for each edge
uv\in E(J)
holds true;

(ii) the maximal degree
\Delta(J)=\Delta
;

(iii) the edge number
|E(J)|\geq r
;

(iii) the graph
J
contains a path
P
of length no less than
d
.

Figure 22:
Examples for illustrating Theorem
40
under the graceful-difference constraint.

Problem 14
.

Let
\varepsilon\in\{ema,edi,fdi,gdi\}
, and let
G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
be the set of graphs, such that each graph
G\in G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
admits a proper total coloring

f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow S_{\varepsilon}(m)

(160)

holding the
\varepsilon
-constraint
m=\varepsilon\langle a,b,c\rangle
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
with
f(u)=a
,
f(v)=b
and
f(uv)=c
about four sets
S_{ema}(m)
,
S_{edi}(m)
,
S_{fdi}(m)
and
S_{gdi}(m)
defined in Theorem
37
, Theorem
38
, Theorem
39
and Theorem
40
, respectively.

Four
-1.

Characterize
each set
S_{\varepsilon}(m)
for each
m\geq 5
and
\varepsilon\in\{ema,edi,fdi,gdi\}
.

Four
-2.

Characterize
some properties of each graph set
G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
for
\varepsilon\in\{ema,edi,fdi,gdi\}
.

Four
-3.

For each group of the
\varepsilon
-constraints
m=\varepsilon\langle a_{i},b_{i},c_{i}\rangle
with
i\in[1,A]
in
S_{\varepsilon}(m)
,
does
there exist a connected
(p,A)
-graph
G\in G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
admitting a proper total coloring
\varphi:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow S_{\varepsilon}(m)
, such that each edge
x_{i}y_{i}\in E(G)
with
i\in[1,A]
just holding
\varphi(x_{i})=a_{i}
,
\varphi(y_{i})=b_{i}
and
\varphi(x_{i}y_{i})=c_{i}
true? Moreover, considering the following particular cases:

(i)
a_{i},b_{i}
and
c_{i}
are prime numbers;

(ii)
a_{i},b_{i}
and
c_{i}
are odd numbers;

(iii)
c_{i}=\textrm{gcd}(a_{i},b_{i})
.

Four
-4.

Find
a connected graph
H\in G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
holding

\frac{|E(H)|}{|V(H)|}\geq\frac{|E(T)|}{|V(T)|}

for any connected graph
T\in G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
with
\varepsilon\in\{ema,edi,fdi,gdi\}
.

Four
-5.

For each graph
G\in G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
,
find
two proper total colorings
f^{v}_{\min}
and
f^{v}_{\max}
(resp.
f^{e}_{\min}
and
f^{e}_{\max}
) defined in Eq.(
160
), such that cardinalities

|f^{v}_{\min}(V(G))|\leq|f(V(G))|\leq|f^{v}_{\max}(V(G))|~(\textrm{resp.}|f^{e}_{\min}(E(G))|\leq|f(E(G))|\leq|f^{e}_{\max}(E(G))|)

(161)

over all proper total colorings
f
of
G
defined in Eq.(
160
).

Four
-6.

Find
a proper total coloring
f^{ve}
for each graph
G\in G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
such that

\frac{|f(E(G))|}{|f(V(G))|}\leq\frac{|f^{ve}(E(G))|}{|f^{ve}(V(G))|}

over all proper total colorings
f
of
G
defined in Eq.(
160
).

Four
-7.

Find
some connections between
S_{\varepsilon}(m)
and
S_{\varepsilon}(n)
(resp.
G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
and
G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(n)
) for
m\neq n
and
\varepsilon\in\{ema,edi,fdi,gdi\}
.

Four
-8.

Find
non-empty subsets
X^{sub}_{graph}\subseteq G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)\cap G^{~\gamma}_{raph}(m)
for distinct
\varepsilon,\gamma\in\{ema
,
edi
,
fdi
,
gdi\}
, because it is guaranteed by Theorem
8
(Ref.
[
34
]
).

Theorem 41
.

∗
By the graph sets
G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
with
\varepsilon\in\{ema,edi,fdi,gdi\}
defined in Problem
14
, we have:

(i) If a connected non-tree
(p,q)
-graph
G^{*}\in G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
admits a proper total coloring
\pi:V(G^{*})\cup E(G^{*})\rightarrow S_{\varepsilon}(m)
, such that each edge
uv\in E(G^{*})
holds the
\varepsilon
-constraint
m=\varepsilon\langle a,b,c\rangle
true, then there is another connected
(p\,^{\prime},q)
-graph
H\in G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
admitting a proper total coloring
\varphi:V(H)\cup E(H)\rightarrow\pi(V(G^{*})\cup E(G^{*}))
holding the
\varepsilon
-constraint
m=\varepsilon\langle a,b,c\rangle
true.

(ii) Each graph
H\in G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
holding the
\varepsilon
-constraint
m=\varepsilon\langle a,b,c\rangle
corresponds to another graph
H^{*}\in G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(km)
with
k\geq 2
, since the
\varepsilon
-constraint
m=\varepsilon\langle a,b,c\rangle
corresponds to the
\varepsilon
-constraint
km=\varepsilon\langle ka,kb,kc\rangle
.

(iii) Each graph
H\in G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m)
holding the
\varepsilon
-constraint
m=\varepsilon\langle a,b,c\rangle
corresponds to another graph
H^{*}\in G^{~\varepsilon}_{raph}(m^{*})
admitting a proper total coloring
g:V(H^{*})\cup E(H^{*})\rightarrow S_{\varepsilon}(m^{*})
holding the
\varepsilon
-constraint

m^{*}=m+k=\varepsilon\langle a+k,b+k,c+k\rangle\textrm{ or }m^{*}=|m-k|=\varepsilon\langle a+k,b+k,c+k\rangle

Remark 26
.

Theorem
8
shows that a connected graph
G
may admit two or more
\varepsilon
-constraint proper total colorings with
\varepsilon
-constraint
\in\{
ema
-constraint
m=ema\langle a,b,c\rangle=a+b+c
,
edi
-constraint
m=edi\langle a,b,c\rangle=c+|a-b|
,
fdi
-constraint
m=fdi\langle a,b,c\rangle=|a+b-c|
,
gdi
-constraint
m=gdi\langle a,b,c\rangle=\big||a-b|-c\big|
\}
.
\parallel

Theorem 42
.

∗
Suppose that a bipartite connected graph
G
admits a proper total coloring
f
holding the
ema
-constraint
m_{1}=ema\langle a,b,c\rangle=a+b+c
and another proper total coloring
g
holding the
edi
-constraint
m_{2}=edi\langle a,b,c\rangle=c+|a-b|
, such that
f(X)<f(Y)
and
g(X)<g(Y)
with
V(G)=X\cup Y
and
X\cap Y=\emptyset
. We make a new proper total coloring
F=f+g
for the connected graph
G
, then

F(x)+F(y)+F(xy)+F(xy)+|F(x)-F(y)|\leq 2(m_{1}+m_{2})

(162)

for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.

Proof.

By Case A shown in the subsection “Connections between magic-constraint colorings of graphs”, we have

{\begin{split}F(x)+F(y)+F(xy)&=f(x)+f(y)+f(xy)+g(x)+g(y)+g(xy)\\
&=m_{1}+g(x)+g(y)+g(xy)\\
&=m_{1}+m_{2}+2g(x)\end{split}}

(163)

By Case B shown in the subsection “Connections between magic-constraint colorings of graphs”, we have

{\begin{split}F(xy)+|F(x)-F(y)|&=(f(xy)+|f(x)-f(y)|)+(g(xy)+|g(x)-g(y)|)\\
&=f(xy)+|f(x)-f(y)|+m_{2}\\
&=m_{1}-2g(x)+m_{2}\end{split}}

(164)

So, Eq.(
162
) follows Eq.(
163
) and Eq.(
164
).
∎

Remark 27
.

About Theorem
42
we have other cases as follows:

Case (i)
The proper total coloring
f
holds the
ema
-constraint
m_{1}=ema\langle a,b,c\rangle=a+b+c
and, the proper total coloring
g
holds the
fdi
-constraint
m_{3}=fdi\langle a,b,c\rangle=|a+b-c|
, and
f(X)<f(Y)
and
g(X)<g(Y)
with
V(G)=X\cup Y
and
X\cap Y=\emptyset
.

By Case A shown in the subsection “Connections between magic-constraint colorings of graphs”, we have

{\begin{split}F(x)+F(y)+F(xy)&=f(x)+f(y)+f(xy)+g(x)+g(y)+g(xy)\\
&=m_{1}+g(x)+g(y)+g(xy)\\
&=m_{1}+m_{3}-2g(xy)\end{split}}

(165)

By Case C shown in the subsection “Connections between magic-constraint colorings of graphs”, we have

{\begin{split}|F(x)+F(y)-F(xy)|&=|(f(x)+f(y)-f(xy))+(g(x)+g(y)-g(xy))|\\
&\leq|f(x)+f(y)-f(xy)|+m_{3}\\
&=m_{3}+m_{1}+2g(xy)\end{split}}

(166)

We get

F(x)+F(y)+F(xy)+|F(x)+F(y)-F(xy)|\leq 2(m_{1}+m_{3})

for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.

Case (ii)
The proper total coloring
f
holds the
ema
-constraint
m_{1}=ema\langle a,b,c\rangle=a+b+c
and, the proper total coloring
g
holds the
gdi
-constraint
m_{4}=gdi\langle a,b,c\rangle=\big||a-b|-c\big|
, and
f(X)<f(Y)
and
g(X)<g(Y)
with
V(G)=X\cup Y
and
X\cap Y=\emptyset
.

By Case A shown in the subsection “Connections between magic-constraint colorings of graphs”, we have

{\begin{split}F(x)+F(y)+F(xy)&=f(x)+f(y)+f(xy)+g(x)+g(y)+g(xy)\\
&=m_{1}+g(x)+g(y)+g(xy)\\
&=m_{1}+m_{4}-2g(y)\end{split}}

(167)

By Case D shown in the subsection “Connections between magic-constraint colorings of graphs”, we have

{\begin{split}\big||F(x)-F(y)|-F(xy)\big|&\leq\big||f(x)-f(y)|-f(xy)\big|+\big||g(x)-g(y)|-g(xy)\big|\\
&=\big||f(x)-f(y)|-f(xy)\big|+m_{4}\\
&=m_{4}+m_{1}-2g(y)\end{split}}

(168)

We obtain

F(x)+F(y)+F(xy)+\big||F(x)-F(y)|-F(xy)\big|\leq 2(m_{1}+m_{4})

for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.

Case (iii)
The proper total coloring
f
holds the
edi
-constraint
m_{2}=edi\langle a,b,c\rangle=c+|a-b|
and, the proper total coloring
g
holds the
fdi
-constraint
m_{3}=fdi\langle a,b,c\rangle=|a+b-c|
, and
f(X)<f(Y)
and
g(X)<g(Y)
with
V(G)=X\cup Y
and
X\cap Y=\emptyset
. Since

{\begin{split}F(xy)+|F(y)-F(x)|&=f(xy)+g(xy)+|[f(x)-f(y)]+[g(x)-g(y)]|\\
&\leq f(xy)+g(xy)+|[f(x)-f(y)]|+|[g(x)-g(y)]|\\
&=m_{2}+g(xy)+|g(x)-g(y)|\\
&=m_{2}+m_{3}+2g(y)\end{split}}

and

{\begin{split}|F(y)+F(x)-F(xy)|&=|[f(x)+f(y)-f(xy)]+[g(x)+g(y)-g(xy)]|\\
&\leq|f(x)+f(y)-f(xy)|+|g(x)+g(y)-g(xy)|\\
&=|f(x)+f(y)-f(xy)|+m_{3}\\
&=m_{2}-2f(xy)+m_{3}\end{split}}

so we obtain

{\begin{split}F(xy)+|F(y)-F(x)|+|F(y)+F(x)-F(xy)|\leq 2(m_{2}+m_{3})\end{split}}

for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.

Case (iv)
The proper total coloring
f
holds the
edi
-constraint
m_{2}=edi\langle a,b,c\rangle=c+|a-b|
and, the proper total coloring
g
holds the
gdi
-constraint
m_{4}=gdi\langle a,b,c\rangle=\big||a-b|-c\big|
, and
f(X)<f(Y)
and
g(X)<g(Y)
with
V(G)=X\cup Y
and
X\cap Y=\emptyset
. Because of

{\begin{split}F(xy)+|F(y)-F(x)|&=f(xy)+g(xy)+|[f(x)-f(y)]+[g(x)-g(y)]|\\
&\leq m_{2}+g(xy)+|g(x)-g(y)|\\
&=m_{2}+m_{4}+2g(xy)\end{split}}

and

{\begin{split}\big||F(x)-F(y)|-F(xy)\big|&\leq\big||f(x)-f(y)|-f(xy)\big|+\big||g(x)-g(y)|-g(xy)\big|\\
&=\big||f(x)-f(y)|-f(xy)\big|+m_{4}\\
&=m_{2}-2f(xy)+m_{4}\end{split}}

so we obtain

F(xy)+|F(y)-F(x)|+\big||F(x)-F(y)|-F(xy)\big|\leq 2(m_{2}+m_{4})

for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.

Case (v)
The proper total coloring
f
holds the
fdi
-constraint
m_{3}=fdi\langle a,b,c\rangle=|a+b-c|
and, the proper total coloring
g
holds the
gdi
-constraint
m_{4}=gdi\langle a,b,c\rangle=\big||a-b|-c\big|
, and
f(X)<f(Y)
and
g(X)<g(Y)
with
V(G)=X\cup Y
and
X\cap Y=\emptyset
. Notice

{\begin{split}|F(y)+F(x)-F(xy)|&\leq|f(y)+f(x)-f(xy)|+|g(y)+g(x)-g(xy)|\\
&=m_{3}+|g(y)+g(x)-g(xy)|\\
&=m_{3}+m_{4}+2g(x)\end{split}}

and

{\begin{split}\big||F(x)-F(y)|-F(xy)\big|&\leq\big||f(x)-f(y)|-f(xy)\big|+\big||g(x)-g(y)|-g(xy)\big|\\
&=\big||f(x)-f(y)|-f(xy)\big|+m_{4}\\
&=m_{3}-2f(x)+m_{4}\end{split}}

then we get

|F(y)+F(x)-F(xy)|+\big||F(x)-F(y)|-F(xy)\big|\leq 2(m_{3}+m_{4})

for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.
\parallel

5.5
Integer-partitioned and integer-decomposed strings

Definition 42
.

∗
Suppose that a positive integer
m
can be partitioned into a sum

m=m_{i,1}+m_{i,2}+\cdots+m_{i,a_{i}},~m_{i,j}>0,~a_{i}\geq 2

(169)

for
i\in[1,P_{art}(m)]
, where
P_{art}(m)
is the number of all possible partitions of the positive integer
m
. Then we have:

(i) Directly, there are
m
-partitioned number-based strings

s^{*}_{i}=m_{i,1}m_{i,2}\dots m_{i,a_{i}},~i\in[1,P_{art}(m)]

(170)

(ii) If a number
m_{i,j}
appeared in Eq.(
169
) can be partitioned into a sum

m_{i,j}=b_{i,j,1}+b_{i,j,2}+\cdots+b_{i,j,s(i,j)},~b_{i,j,s}>0,~s(i,j)\geq 2

(171)

then we get a
compound
m
-partitioned number-based string

s^{j}_{i}=m_{i,1}m_{i,2}\dots m_{i,j-1}~b_{i,j,1}b_{i,j,2}\dots b_{i,j,s(i,j)}~m_{i,j+1}\dots m_{i,a_{i}}

(172)

(iii) Let
m_{i,j,1},m_{i,j,2},\dots,m_{i,j,a_{i}}
be the
j
th permutation of the numbers
m_{i,1},m_{i,2},\dots,m_{i,a_{i}}
for
j\in[1,(a_{i})!]
and
i\in[1,P_{art}(m)]
. We have
m
-partitioned number-based strings

s_{i,j}=m_{i,j,1}m_{i,j,2}\cdots m_{i,j,a_{i}},~j\in[1,(a_{i})!],~i\in[1,P_{art}(m)]

(173)

There are
\prod^{P_{art}(m)}_{i=1}(a_{i})!
different
m
-partitioned number-based strings of form
s_{i,j}
defined in Eq.(
173
).
\square

Problem 15
.

Given a number-based string
s=c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{n}
with
c_{i}\in[0,9]
and
c_{1}\geq 1
,
find
a positive integer
m
, such that
m
can be expressed as a sum
m=m_{i,1}+m_{i,2}+\cdots+m_{i,a_{i}}
defined in Eq.(
169
), and there is
s=s^{*}_{i}
defined in Eq.(
170
), or
s=s^{j}_{i}
defined in Eq.(
172
), or
s=s_{i,j}
defined in Eq.(
173
).

Definition 43
.

∗
Suppose that a positive integer
m
can be decomposed into a product

n=p_{i,1}\cdot p_{i,2}\cdot\cdots\cdot p_{i,b_{i}},~p_{i,j}\geq 3,~b_{i}\geq 2,~i\in[1,D_{com}(n)]

(174)

where
D_{com}(n)
is the number of all possible decompositions of the positive integer
n
. We have:

(i) there are
n
-decomposed number-based strings

r^{*}_{i}=p_{i,1}\cdot p_{i,2}\cdot\dots\cdot p_{i,b_{i}},~i\in[1,D_{com}(n)]

(175)

(ii) If a number
p_{i,j}
appeared in Eq.(
174
) can be decomposed into a product

p_{i,j}=c_{i,j,1}\cdot c_{i,j,2}\cdot\cdots\cdot c_{i,j,t(i,j)},~c_{i,j,s}>0,~t(i,j)\geq 2

(176)

then we get a
compound
n
-decomposed number-based string

r^{j}_{i}=p_{i,1}\cdot p_{i,2}\cdot\dots\cdot p_{i,j-1}~\cdot c_{i,j,1}\cdot c_{i,j,2}\cdot\dots\cdot c_{i,j,s(i,j)}\cdot~p_{i,j+1}\dots\cdot p_{i,b_{i}}

(177)

(iii) Let
p_{i,j,1},p_{i,j,2},\dots,p_{i,j,b_{i}}
be the
j
th permutation of the numbers
p_{i,1},p_{i,2},\dots,p_{i,b_{i}}
for
j\in[1,(b_{i})!]
and
i\in[1,D_{com}(n)]
. We have
n
-decomposed number-based strings

r_{i,j}=p_{i,j,1}\cdot p_{i,j,2}\cdot\cdots\cdot p_{i,j,b_{i}},~j\in[1,(b_{i})!],~i\in[1,D_{com}(n)]

(178)

In general, there are
\prod^{D_{com}(n)}_{i=1}(b_{i})!
different
m
-decomposed number-based strings of form
r_{i,j}
defined in Eq.(
178
).
\square

Problem 16
.

Given a number-based string
s^{*}=a_{1}a_{2}\cdots a_{m}
with
a_{i}\in[0,9]
and
a_{1}\geq 1
,
find
a positive integer
n
, such that
n
can be expressed as a product
n=p_{i,1}p_{i,2}\cdots p_{i,b_{i}}
defined in Eq.(
174
), and there is
s^{*}=r^{*}_{i}
defined in Eq.(
175
), or
s^{*}=r^{j}_{i}
defined in Eq.(
177
), or
s^{*}=r_{i,j}
defined in Eq.(
178
).

Example 25
.

We show some number-based strings as
public-key strings
and
private-key strings
made by the integer partition and the integer decomposition.

(i)
The twin-sum string authentication
. In Eq.(
170
), two numbers
m_{i,j}
and
m_{i,j+1}
are called
twin numbers
, by Eq.(
171
) and the following Eq.(
179
)

m_{i,j+1}=b_{i,j+1,1}+b_{i,j+1,2}+\cdots+b_{i,j+1,s(i,j+1)},~b_{i,j+1,s}>0,~s(i,j+1)\geq 2

(179)

we have a
public-key string

s^{j}_{i}
shown in Eq.(
172
) and the following
private-key string

s^{j+1}_{i}=m_{i,1}m_{i,2}\cdots m_{i,j}~b_{i,j+1,1}b_{i,j+1,2}\cdots b_{i,j+1,s(i,j+1)}~m_{i,j+2}\cdots m_{i,a_{i}}

(180)

Finally, the following number-based string

{\begin{split}m_{i,1}m_{i,2}\cdots m_{i,j-1}~b_{i,j,1}b_{i,j,2}\cdots b_{i,j,s(i,j)}~b_{i,j+1,1}b_{i,j+1,2}\cdots b_{i,j+1,s(i,j+1)}~m_{i,j+2}\cdots m_{i,a_{i}}\end{split}}

(181)

is called
twin-sum string authentication
, denoted as
A^{sum}_{uth}\langle s^{j}_{i},s^{j+1}_{i}\rangle
.

(ii)
The twin-product string authentication
. In Eq.(
174
), two numbers
p_{i,j}
and
p_{i,j+1}
are called
twin numbers
, like Eq.(
176
), we have

p_{i,j+1}=c_{i,j+1,1}c_{i,j+1,2}\cdots c_{i,j+1,t(i,j+1)},~c_{i,j+1,s}>0,~t(i,j+1)\geq 2

(182)

Immediately, we get the following number-based string

{\begin{split}A^{pro}_{uth}\langle r^{j}_{i},r^{j+1}_{i}\rangle=&p_{i,1}p_{i,2}\cdots p_{i,j-1}~c_{i,j,1}c_{i,j,2}\cdots c_{i,j,s(i,j)}\\
&c_{i,j+1,1}c_{i,j+1,2}\cdots c_{i,j+1,t(i,j+1)}~p_{i,j+1}\cdots p_{i,b_{i}}\end{split}}

(183)

called
twin-product string authentication
.

(iii)
The twin-mixed string authentication
. By Eq.(
171
), Eq.(
179
), Eq.(
176
) and Eq.(
182
), we have a
twin-mixed string authentication

{\begin{split}A^{mix}_{uth}\langle r^{j}_{i},s^{j+1}_{i}\rangle=&m_{i,1}m_{i,2}\cdots m_{i,j-1}~c_{i,j,1}c_{i,j,2}\cdots c_{i,j,s(i,j)}~m_{i,j+1}\\
&b_{i,j+1,1}b_{i,j+1,2}\cdots b_{i,j+1,s(i,j+1)}~m_{i,j+2}\cdots m_{i,a_{i}}\end{split}}

(184)

where
r^{j}_{i}
is as a
public-key string
and
s^{j+1}_{i}
is as a
private-key string
; and moreover the following number-based string

{\begin{split}A^{mix}_{uth}\langle s^{j}_{i},r^{j+1}_{i}\rangle=&p_{i,1}p_{i,2}\cdots p_{i,j-1}~b_{i,j,1}b_{i,j,2}\cdots b_{i,j,s(i,j)}\\
&c_{i,j+1,1}c_{i,j+1,2}\cdots c_{i,j+1,t(i,j+1)}~p_{i,j+1}\cdots p_{i,b_{i}}\end{split}}

(185)

is called
twin-mixed string authentication
, where
s^{j}_{i}
is as a
public-key string
and
r^{j+1}_{i}
is as a
private-key string
.

(iv)
The
A_{m}
-sum
B_{n}
-product complementary key-pairs
. We take
A
numbers
m_{i,j_{t}}
with
t\in[1,A]
from a sum
m=m_{i,1}+m_{i,2}+\cdots+m_{i,a_{i}}
, and take
B
numbers
p_{i,j_{s}}
with
s\in[1,B]
from a product
n=p_{i,1}p_{i,2}\cdots p_{i,b_{i}}
, and use them to make a
public-key string

s_{pub}(A_{m},B_{n})=\langle m_{i,j_{t}}|^{A}_{t=1},p_{i,j_{s}}|^{B}_{s=1}\rangle

so we have two sets

\overline{S}_{m}=\{m_{i,1},m_{i,2},\dots,m_{i,a_{i}}\}\setminus\{m_{i,j_{t}}:t\in[1,A]\}
and

\overline{P}_{n}=\{p_{i,1},p_{i,2},\dots,p_{i,b_{i}}\}\setminus\{p_{i,j_{s}}:s\in[1,B]\}
,

the elements of them produce a
private-key string

s_{pri}(\overline{A}_{m},\overline{B}_{n})=\langle\overline{S}_{m},\overline{P}_{n}\rangle
. We call
s_{pub}(A_{m},B_{n})
and
s_{pri}(\overline{A}_{m},\overline{B}_{n})
as a
A_{m}
-sum
B_{n}
-product complementary Key-pair
.

It is meaningful to
do
:

(do-1) Analysis the computational complexity of the number-based strings defined above.

(do-2) Make more complex number-based strings by sums
m=m_{i,1}+m_{i,2}+\cdots+m_{i,a_{i}}
and products
n=p_{i,1}p_{i,2}\cdots p_{i,b_{i}}
for Key-pairs.
\square

Remark 28
.

The computational complexity of the Integer Partition Problem.
We partition a positive integer
m
into a group of
a_{i}
parts
m_{i,1},m_{i,2},\dots,m_{i,a_{i}}
holding
m=m_{i,1}+m_{i,2}+\cdots+m_{i,a_{i}}
with each
m_{i,j}>0
and
a_{i}\geq 2
; refer to Eq.(
169
). Suppose there is
P_{art}(m)
groups of such
a_{i}
parts. Computing the number
P_{art}(m)
can be transformed into finding the number
A(m,a_{i})
of solutions of
Diophantine equation

m=\sum^{k}_{i=1}ix_{i}
. There is a recursive formula

A(m,a_{i})=A(m,a_{i}-1)+A(m-a_{i},a_{i})

(186)

with
0\leq a_{i}\leq m
. It is not easy to compute the exact value of
A(m,a_{i})
, for example, the authors in
[
64
]
and
[
65
]
computed exactly

{\begin{split}A(m,6)=&\biggr\lfloor\frac{1}{1036800}(12m^{5}+270m^{4}+1520m^{3}-1350m^{2}-19190m-9081)+\\
&\frac{(-1)^{m}(m^{2}+9m+7)}{768}+\frac{1}{81}\left[(m+5)\cos\frac{2m\pi}{3}\right]\biggr\rfloor\end{split}}

On the other hands, for any odd integer
m\geq 7
, it was conjectured
m=p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}
with three primes
p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}
from the famous Goldbach’s conjecture: “
Every even integer, greater than 2, can be expressed as the sum of two primes
.” In other word, determining
A(m,3)
is difficult, also, it is difficult to express an odd integer
m=\sum^{3n}_{k=1}p\,^{\prime}_{k}
with each
p\,^{\prime}_{k}
is a prime number.

Clearly, it is difficult to partition an integer
m
into
m=m_{i,1}+m_{i,2}+\cdots+m_{i,a_{i}}
defined in Eq.(
169
), or decompose an integer
n
as
n=p_{i,1}\cdot p_{i,2}\cdot\cdots\cdot p_{i,b_{i}}
defined in Eq.(
174
).
\parallel

5.6
Graphic lattices for making number-based strings

5.6.1
A series of operation graphic lattices

Let
\textbf{{O}}=(O_{1},O_{2},\dots,O_{p})
be a
graph operation base
, where each
O_{i}
is a graph operation of graph theory, and let
\textbf{{T}}_{i}=\big(T_{i,1},T_{i,2},\dots,T_{i,n_{i}}\big)
be a
colored graph base
for
n_{i}\geq 1
and
i\in[1,M]
, so that each colored graph
T_{i,j}
admits a total coloring (or a total graph-coloring)
f_{i,j}
. Suppose that each colored graph base
\textbf{{T}}_{i}
is an every-zero additive group defined in Definition
67
. By Eq.(
123
), Eq.(
123
) and Eq.(
123
) in the TOTAL-graph-coloring algorithm-II with graph operation, there is a series of
operation graphic lattices
.

First operation graphic lattice is

\textbf{{L}}(\textbf{{Q}}_{1}[\textbf{{O}}]\textbf{{T}}_{1})=\left\{H[\textbf{{O}}]^{n_{1}}_{k=1}a_{1,k}T_{1,k}:~a_{1,k}\in Z^{0},~T_{1,k}\in\textbf{{T}}_{1},H\in\textbf{{Q}}_{1}\right\}

(187)

with
\sum^{n_{1}}_{k=1}a_{1,k}\geq 1
, where
\textbf{{Q}}_{1}
is a graph set.

Let
\textbf{{Q}}_{2}=\textbf{{L}}(\textbf{{Q}}_{1}[\textbf{{O}}]\textbf{{T}}_{1})
, the second operation graphic lattice is

\textbf{{L}}(\textbf{{Q}}_{2}[\textbf{{O}}]\textbf{{T}}_{2})=\big\{H[\textbf{{O}}]^{n_{2}}_{k=1}a_{2,k}T_{2,k}:~a_{2,k}\in Z^{0},~T_{2,k}\in\textbf{{T}}_{2},H\in\textbf{{Q}}_{2}\big\}

(188)

with
\sum^{n_{2}}_{k=1}a_{2,k}\geq 1
.

In general, the
s
th operation graphic lattice is

\textbf{{L}}(\textbf{{Q}}_{s+1}[\textbf{{O}}]\textbf{{T}}_{s+1})=\big\{H[\textbf{{O}}]^{n_{s+1}}_{k=1}a_{s+1,k}T_{s+1,k}:~a_{s+1,k}\in Z^{0},~T_{s+1,k}\in\textbf{{T}}_{s+1},H\in\textbf{{Q}}_{s+1}\big\}

(189)

with
\sum^{n_{s+1}}_{k=1}a_{s+1,k}\geq 1
, and
\textbf{{Q}}_{s+1}=\textbf{{L}}(\textbf{{Q}}_{s}[\textbf{{O}}]\textbf{{T}}_{s})
for
s\geq 1
.

5.6.2
Tree-base graphic lattices

In
[
34
]
, a
tree base

\textbf{{T}}=(T_{1},T_{2},\dots,T_{m})
consists of mutually vertex-disjoint uncolored trees
T_{1},T_{2},\dots,T_{m}
holding
T_{i}\not\cong T_{j}
if
i\neq j
. We use the edges of an edge set
E^{*}
to join these mutually disjoint trees together, the resulting graph is denoted as
\textbf{{T}}+E^{*}
. Notice that one tree
T_{i}
may be used two or more times in
\textbf{{T}}+E^{*}
, so we write
E^{*}[\ominus]^{n}_{k=1}\alpha_{k}T_{k}
with
\sum^{m}_{k=1}\alpha_{k}\geq 1
for replacing
\textbf{{T}}+E^{*}
by the
edge-join operation
“
[\ominus]
”. We have a
tree-base graphic lattice
as follows:

\textbf{{L}}(\textbf{{E}}[\ominus]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})=\big\{E^{*}[\ominus]^{m}_{k=1}\mu_{k}T_{k}:~\mu_{k}\in Z^{0},T_{k}\in\textbf{{T}},E^{*}\in\textbf{{E}}\big\}

(190)

with
\sum^{m}_{k=1}\mu_{k}\geq 1
.

Let
J_{i_{1}},J_{i_{2}},\dots,J_{i_{M}}
be a permutation of the trees
\lambda_{1}T_{1}
,
\lambda_{2}T_{2}
,
\dots
,
\lambda_{m}T_{m}
for
M=\sum^{m}_{k=1}\lambda_{k}\geq 1
. By the vertex-coinciding operation “
[\odot]
”, we vertex-coincide some vertices
x_{i_{j},1},x_{i_{j},2},\dots,x_{i_{j},s}
of the tree
J_{i_{j}}
with some vertices
y_{i_{j+1},1},y_{i_{j+1},2},\dots,y_{i_{j+1},t}
of the tree
J_{i_{j+1}}
into

\{x_{i_{j},1},x_{i_{j},2},\dots,x_{i_{j},s}\}\odot\{y_{i_{j+1},1},y_{i_{j+1},2},\dots,y_{i_{j+1},t}\},~j\in[1,M-1]

the resultant graph is denoted as

J_{i_{1}}[\odot]J_{i_{2}}[\odot]\dots[\odot]J_{i_{M}}=[\odot]^{M}_{j=1}J_{i_{j}}=[\odot]^{m}_{k=1}a_{k}T_{k}

(191)

which induces a
tree-base graphic lattice

\textbf{{L}}([\odot]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})=\big\{[\odot]^{m}_{k=1}\lambda_{k}T_{k}:~\lambda_{k}\in Z^{0},T_{k}\in\textbf{{T}}\big\}

(192)

with
\sum^{m}_{k=1}\lambda_{k}\geq 1
.

Problem 17
.

About tree-base graphic lattices defined in Eq.(
192
),
is
there

\textbf{{L}}([\odot]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})\cap\textbf{{L}}([\odot]Z^{0}\textbf{{H}})\neq\emptyset

for different tree-bases
T
and
H
?

We do the
edge-join operation
“
[\ominus]
” and the vertex-coinciding operation “
[\odot]
” to a permutation
H_{i_{1}},H_{i_{2}},\dots,H_{i_{M}}
of the trees
\gamma_{1}T_{1}
,
\gamma_{2}T_{2}
,
\dots
,
\gamma_{m}T_{m}
for
M=\sum^{m}_{k=1}\gamma_{k}\geq 1
, and write the resultant graphs as
[\ominus\odot]^{m}_{k=1}\gamma_{k}T_{k}
, immediately, we get a
tree-base graphic lattice

\textbf{{L}}([\ominus\odot]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})=\big\{[\ominus\odot]^{m}_{k=1}\gamma_{k}T_{k}:~\gamma_{k}\in Z^{0},T_{k}\in\textbf{{T}}\big\}

(193)

with
\sum^{m}_{k=1}\gamma_{k}\geq 1
.

All-tree-graphic lattice.
For considering the coloring closure of graphic lattices, we let
\textbf{{L}}_{\textrm{tree}}(\textbf{{E}}[\ominus]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
be the set of all trees in a tree-base graphic lattice
\textbf{{L}}(\textbf{{E}}[\ominus]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
defined in Eq.(
190
), and we call it
all-tree-graphic lattice
. Similarly, we have other two all-tree-graphic lattices

\textbf{{L}}_{\textrm{tree}}([\odot]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})\subset\textbf{{L}}([\odot]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}}),~\textbf{{L}}_{\textrm{tree}}([\ominus\odot]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})\subset\textbf{{L}}([\ominus\odot]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})

defined in Eq.(
192
) and Eq.(
193
), respectively.

By Theorem
8
, these three all-tree-graphic lattices are
coloring closure
to the
(k,d)
-total colorings stated in the following theorem
43
:

Theorem 43
.

[
34
]
Each tree contained in
\textbf{{L}}_{\textrm{tree}}(\textbf{{E}}[\ominus]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
,
\textbf{{L}}_{\textrm{tree}}([\odot]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
and
\textbf{{L}}_{\textrm{tree}}([\ominus\odot]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
admits the following
(k,d)
-total colorings: graceful
(k,d)
-total coloring, harmonious
(k,d)
-total coloring, (odd-edge) edge-difference
(k,d)
-total coloring, (odd-edge) graceful-difference
(k,d)
-total coloring, (odd-edge) felicitous-difference
(k,d)
-total coloring, (odd-edge) edge-magic
(k,d)
-total coloring.

5.6.3
Tree-base
[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]
-operation graphic lattices

Let
\textbf{{T}}=(T_{1},T_{2},\dots,T_{m})
be a
tree base
, where the mutually vertex-disjoint uncolored trees
T_{1},T_{2}
,
\dots
,
T_{m}
holds
T_{i}\not\cong T_{j}
if
i\neq j
.

Tree-base
[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]
-operation graphic lattice.
A
proper vertex-coinciding operation
“
[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]
” to the trees of the tree base
T
produces graphs
H=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{n}_{j=1}T_{j}
holding:

(a-1) there is a graph homomorphism
\big\langle\bigcup^{m}_{j=1}T_{j}\big\rangle\rightarrow H
;

(a-2)
H
is connected;

(a-3)
H
has no multiple-edge, and
E(H)=\bigcup^{m}_{j=1}E(T_{j})
.

Moreover, we take a permutation
L_{i_{1}},L_{i_{2}},\dots,L_{i_{A}}
of the trees
b_{1}T_{1}
,
b_{2}T_{2}
,
\dots
,
b_{m}T_{m}
from the tree base
T
with
A=\sum^{m}_{k=1}b_{k}\geq 1
. Then we get graphs

G=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{A}_{j=1}L_{i_{j}}=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{m}_{k=1}b_{k}T_{k}

(194)

after doing the
proper vertex-coinciding operation
“
[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]
” to the permutation
L_{i_{1}},L_{i_{2}},\dots,L_{i_{A}}
, such that

(b-1) there is a graph homomorphism
\big\langle\bigcup^{A}_{j=1}L_{i_{j}}\big\rangle\rightarrow G
;

(b-2)
G
is connected;

(b-3)
G
has no multiple-edge, and
E(G)=\bigcup^{A}_{j=1}E(L_{i_{j}})
.

Thereby, we get a
tree-base
[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]
-operation graphic lattice

\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})=\big\{[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{m}_{k=1}b_{k}T_{k}:~b_{k}\in Z^{0},T_{k}\in\textbf{{T}}\big\}

(195)

with
\sum^{m}_{k=1}b_{k}\geq 1
.

Problem 18
.

Is
there another tree base
\textbf{{H}}=(H_{1},H_{2},\dots,H_{n})
with
H_{i}\not\cong H_{j}
if
i\neq j
, such that two tree-base
[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]
-operation graphic lattices hold
\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{H}})=\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
true?

Remark 29
.

Clearly, each connected graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
defined in Eq.(
195
) can be vertex-split (also, decompose) into the trees
b_{1}T_{1}
,
b_{2}T_{2}
,
\dots
,
b_{m}T_{m}
from the tree base
T
with
\sum^{m}_{k=1}b_{k}\geq 1
; refer to Problem
4
. There are the following particular cases about the lattices
\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
:

Case-1.
If each tree
T_{i}
in the tree base
T
holds
|V(T_{i})|=p
true, then the connected graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
is
spanning-tree decomposable
as
|V(G)|=p
.

Case-2.
If each tree
T_{i}
in the tree base
T
is a
caterpillar
(resp.
lobster
), then we say that the connected graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
is
caterpillar decomposable
(resp.
lobster decomposable
).

Case-3.

The extremum problems.
Let
\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
be the set of graphs in
\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
for a fixed group of non-negative integers
b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m}
such that each connected graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
holding
G=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{m}_{k=1}b_{k}T_{k}
. Motivated from some traditional lattice problems, we propose the following extremum problems:
Find
a connected graph
H^{*}\in\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
, such that

(i)

the vertex numbers
|V(H^{*})|\leq|V(G)|
for any connected graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
.

(ii)

the diameters
D(H^{*})\leq D(G)
for any connected graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
.

(iii)

the maximal degrees
\Delta(H^{*})\geq\Delta(G)
for any connected graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
.

(iv)

H^{*}
contains a path
P^{*}
holding
|V(P^{*})|\geq|V(P)|
for any path
P
contained in any connected graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
.

(v)

the number of cycles of
H^{*}
is largest in
\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
.

(vi)

the combinatorics of the above extremum cases.

(vii)

determine
the cardinality
|\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})|
.

Case-4.

The set-colorings of connected graphs in
\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
.
Since each tree
T_{i}
with
i\in[1,m]
admits a group of colorings
h_{1},h_{2},\dots,h_{n}
, then each connected
(p,q)
-graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
admits a set-coloring
F=\langle h_{1},h_{2},\dots,h_{n}\rangle
, consider the following questions:

Scola
-1.

If each
h_{i}
is a set-ordered graceful labeling, then the connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a set-coloring
F=\langle h_{1},h_{2},\dots,h_{n}\rangle
such that the edge color set
F(E(G))=[1,q]
.

Scola
-2.

If each
h_{i}
is a set-ordered odd-graceful labeling, then the connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a set-coloring
F=\langle h_{1},h_{2},\dots,h_{n}\rangle
such that the edge color set
F(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
.

Scola
-3.

If each
h_{i}
is one coloring/labeling defined in Definition
2
, Definition
3
and Definition
4
, what property does the connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
have such that
G
admits a total set-coloring
F
defined by
h_{1}
,
h_{2}
,
\dots
,
h_{n}
?
\parallel

Problem 19
.

For a fixed group of non-negative integers
b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m}
with
\sum^{m}_{k=1}b_{k}\geq 1
, let
\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
be the set of graphs of the graphic lattice
\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
defined in Eq.(
195
).

(i) If
K_{n}=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{m}_{i=1}T_{i}
with
n=|V(T_{i})|
for
i\in[1,m]
in the tree base
T
, then the set
\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
contains graphs with cliques
K_{n}
.
Determine
a connected graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots
,
b_{m})
such that
G
contains the largest number of cliques like
K_{n}
.

(ii) If
K_{m}=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{m}_{k=1}T_{k}
with the vertex number
|V(T_{i})|=k
for
k\in[1,m]
in the tree base
T
, that is
\langle T_{1},T_{2},\dots
,
T_{m}\mid K_{m}\rangle
; refer to Conjecture
5
.
Find
a connected graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
such that
G
contains the most cliques of form
K_{m}
.

Conjecture 5
.

K
-
T

conjecture
(Gyárás and Lehel, 1978; Béla Bollobás, 1995): For integer
n\geq 3
, given
n
mutually disjoint trees
T_{k}
of
k
vertices with respect to
1\leq k\leq n
. Then the complete graph
K_{n}
can be decomposed into the union of
n
mutually edge-disjoint trees
H_{k}
, namely
K_{n}=\bigcup^{n}_{k=1}H_{k}
, such that
T_{k}\cong H_{k}
whenever
1\leq k\leq n
. Also, write this case as
\langle T_{1},T_{2},\dots,T_{n}\mid K_{n}\rangle
.

Definition 44
.

[
35
]
Suppose that a complete graph
K_{n}
admits a total coloring
f:V(K_{n})\cup E(K_{n})\rightarrow[1,n]
holding
|f(V(K_{n}))|=n
and
f(E(K_{n}))=\{f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|:uv\in E(K_{n})\}
. Doing the vertex-split operation to
K_{n}
produces a group of trees
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3},\dots,T_{n}
, where each tree
T_{k}
has just
k
vertices for
k\in[1,n]
, such that
K_{n}=\odot|^{n}_{k=1}T_{k}
, also
\langle T_{1},T_{2},T_{3},\dots,T_{n}\mid K_{n}\rangle
(refer to Conjecture
5
). We call the set
S=\{T_{1},T_{2},T_{3},\dots,T_{n}\}
a
vertex-split tree-group
of
K_{n}
. Let
f_{k}
be the total coloring of each tree
T_{k}
, so
f_{k}(x)\neq f_{k}(y)
for
x,y\in V(T_{k})
, if

f_{k}(E(T_{k}))=\big\{f_{k}(uv)=|f_{k}(u)-f_{k}(v)|:uv\in E(T_{k})\big\}=[1,k-1]

we call this set
S

totally graceful vertex-split tree-group
of
K_{n}
.

\square

Figure 23:
Seven totally graceful vertex-split tree-groups of
K_{5}
for understanding Definition
44
, cited from
[
35
]
.

Problem 20
.

Does
each complete graph
K_{n}
have a totally graceful vertex-split tree-group defined in Definition
44
?

Definition 45
.

[
37
]
A
W
-constraint v-set e-proper total coloring
of a connected graph
G
is a mapping
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow\Omega
, where
\Omega
consists of numbers and sets, such that

(i)
f(u)
for each vertex
u\in V(G)
is a set;

(ii)
f(xy)
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
is a number
c
; and

(iii) there are some
a\in f(u)
and
b\in f(v)
holding the
W
-constraint
c=W\langle a,b\rangle
.
\square

By Theorem
3
and Theorem
8
, we have

Theorem 44
.

∗
Each complete graph
K_{2m}
of
2m
vertices admits a graceful v-set e-proper total coloring
f
such that the edge set
E(K_{2m})=\bigcup^{m}_{k=1}E_{k}
, and there are some
a_{u}\in f(u)
and
b_{v}\in f(v)
, and
f(uv)=k=|a_{u}-b_{v}|
for each edge
uv\in E_{k}
with
k\in[1,m]
.

Theorem 45
.

∗
For a fixed group of non-negative integers
b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m}
with
B=\sum^{m}_{k=1}b_{k}\geq 1
, let
\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
be the set of graphs in the lattice
\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
defined in Eq.(
195
) such that each connected
(p,q)
-graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
holds
G=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{m}_{k=1}b_{k}T_{k}
true. If each tree
T_{i}
of the lattice base
T
admits a
graceful
(k,d)
-total coloring

f_{i}
for
i\in[1,m]
, then each connected
(p,q)
-graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
admits at least
B!
different
graceful v-set e-proper colorings

F_{k}
such that each edge color set
F_{k}(E(G))=[1,q]
for
k\in[1,B!]
.

Proof.

By Theorem
8
, each tree
T
with diameter
D(T)\geq 3
admits at least
2^{m}
different graceful
(k,d)
-total colorings for
m+1=\Big\lceil\frac{D(T)}{2}\Big\rceil
.

Notice that each tree
T_{i}\in\textbf{{T}}
is a bipartite and connected graph with its own vertex set
V(T_{i})=X_{i}\cup Y_{i}
and
X_{i}\cap Y_{i}=\emptyset
. According to Definition
2
, a graceful
(k,d)
-total coloring
f_{i}
of each tree
T_{i}
is defined as

{\begin{split}&f_{i}:X_{i}\rightarrow S_{m,0,0,d}=\{0,d,\dots,md\},\\
&f_{i}:Y_{i}\cup E(T_{i})\rightarrow S_{q_{i}-1,k,0,d}=\{k,k+d,\dots,k+(q_{i}-1)d\}\end{split}}

(196)

where
q_{i}=|E(T_{i})|
, and it is allowed
f_{i}(u)=f_{i}(w)
for some distinct vertices
u,w\in V(T_{i})
, such that the edge color set

f(E(T_{i}))=\{f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|:uv\in E(T_{i})\}=\{k,k+d,\dots,k+(q_{i}-1)d\}

(197)

For a permutation
H_{i_{1}},H_{i_{2}},\dots,H_{i_{B}}
of the trees
b_{1}T_{1}
,
b_{2}T_{2}
,
\dots
,
b_{m}T_{m}
from the tree base
T
with
B=\sum^{m}_{k=1}b_{k}\geq 1
, we have connected graphs

G=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{B}_{j=1}H_{i_{j}}=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{m}_{k=1}b_{k}T_{k}

(198)

and each tree
H_{i_{j}}
admits a graceful
(k_{i_{j}},d_{i_{j}})
-total coloring
g_{i_{j}}
defined as

{\begin{split}&g_{i_{j}}:X_{i_{j}}\rightarrow S_{m_{i_{j}},0,0,d_{i_{j}}}=\big\{0,d_{i_{j}},\dots,m_{i_{j}}d_{i_{j}}\big\},\\
&g_{i_{j}}:Y_{i_{j}}\cup E(H_{i_{j}})\rightarrow S_{q_{i_{j}}-1,k_{i_{j}},0,d_{i_{j}}}=\big\{k_{i_{j}},k_{i_{j}}+d_{i_{j}},\dots,k_{i_{j}}+(q_{i_{j}}-1)d_{i_{j}}\big\}\end{split}}

(199)

where
q_{i_{j}}=|E(H_{i_{j}})|
and the vertex set
V(H_{i_{j}})=X_{i_{j}}\cup Y_{i_{j}}
with
X_{i_{j}}\cap Y_{i_{j}}=\emptyset
, and it is allowed
g_{i_{j}}(x)=g_{i_{j}}(u)
for some distinct vertices
x,u\in V(T_{i})
, such that the edge color set

{\begin{split}g_{i_{j}}(E(H_{i_{j}}))&=\big\{g_{i_{j}}(uv)=|g_{i_{j}}(u)-g_{i_{j}}(v)|:uv\in E(H_{i_{j}})\big\}\\
&=\big\{k_{i_{j}},k_{i_{j}}+d_{i_{j}},\dots,k_{i_{j}}+(q_{i_{j}}-1)d_{i_{j}}\big\}\end{split}}

(200)

Let
\Sigma(j)=\sum^{j}_{k=1}q_{i_{k}}
for
j\in[1,B]
. For
(k_{i_{1}},d_{i_{1}})=(1,1)
, we get the edge color set

g_{i_{1}}(E(H_{i_{1}}))=\big\{1,2,\dots,q_{i_{1}}\big\}=[1,q_{i_{1}}]=[1,\Sigma(1)]

As
(k_{i_{2}},d_{i_{2}})=(\Sigma(1)+1,1)
, we get the edge color set

g_{i_{2}}(E(H_{i_{2}}))=\big\{\Sigma(1)+1,\Sigma(1)+2,\dots,\Sigma(1)+1+(q_{i_{2}}-1)\big\}=[\Sigma(1)+1,\Sigma(2)]

In general, we take
(k_{i_{j+1}},d_{i_{j+1}})=(\Sigma(j)+1,1)
, then we get the edge color set

g_{i_{j+1}}(E(H_{i_{j+1}}))=\big\{\Sigma(j)+1,\Sigma(j)+2,\dots,\Sigma(j)+1+(q_{i_{j+1}}-1)\big\}=\left[\Sigma(j)+1,\Sigma(j+1)\right]

For the last
(k_{i_{B}},d_{i_{B}})=(\Sigma(B-1)+1,1)
, we have the edge color set

g_{i_{B}}(E(H_{i_{B}}))=\big\{\Sigma(B-1)+1,\Sigma(B-1)+2,\dots,\Sigma(B-1)+1+(q_{i_{B}}-1)\big\}=\left[\Sigma(B-1)+1,\Sigma(B)\right]

By Eq.(
198
) and the permutation
H_{i_{1}},H_{i_{2}},\dots,H_{i_{B}}
of the trees
b_{1}T_{1},b_{2}T_{2},\dots,b_{m}T_{m}
, the connected graph
G
admits a graceful v-set e-proper coloring
F_{i}=\langle g_{i_{1}},g_{i_{2}},\dots
,
g_{i_{B}}\rangle
defined by setting
g_{i_{j}}(x)\subseteq F_{i}(x)
,
g_{i_{j}}(y)\subseteq F_{i}(y)
and
F_{i}(xy)=g_{i_{j}}(xy)
if edge
xy\in E(H_{i_{j}})\subseteq E(G)
, such that the edge color set

F_{i}(E(G))=\big\{F_{i}(uv)=|F_{i}(u)-F_{i}(v)|:uv\in E(G)\big\}=\bigcup^{B}_{j=1}g_{i_{j}}(E(H_{i_{j}}))=[1,q]

where
F_{i}(uv)=|F_{i}(u)-F_{i}(v)|
stands for
g_{i_{j}}(uv)=|g_{i_{j}}(u)-g_{i_{j}}(v)|
if edge
uv\in E(H_{i_{j}})
.

Since there are
B!
permutations
H_{i_{1}},H_{i_{2}},\dots,H_{i_{B}}
of the trees
b_{1}T_{1},b_{2}T_{2},\dots,b_{m}T_{m}
for
i\in[1,B!]
with
B=\sum^{m}_{k=1}b_{k}\geq 1
, we have shown that the connected graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{m})
admits
B!
different graceful v-set e-proper colorings
f
with the edge color set
f(E(G))=[1,q]
.

The proof of the theorem is complete.
∎

Corollary 46
.

∗
For a fixed group of non-negative integers
a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{m}
with
A=\sum^{m}_{k=1}a_{k}\geq 1
, let
\textbf{{L}}(a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{m})
be the set of graphs in the lattice
\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
defined in Eq.(
195
) such that each connected
(p,q)
-graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{m})
holding
G=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{m}_{k=1}a_{k}T_{k}
. If each tree
T_{i}
in the lattice base
T
admits a
graceful
(k,d)
-total coloring

\varphi_{i}
for
i\in[1,m]
, then each connected
(p,q)
-graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{m})
admits at least
A!
different
W
-constraint v-set e-proper colorings

\theta_{k}=\langle\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\dots,\varphi_{m}\rangle
such that

(i) As
W
-constraint =
harmonious
, so each edge color set
\theta_{k}(E(G))=[1,q]
for
k\in[1,A!]
.

(ii) As
W
-constraint =
odd-graceful
, then each edge color set
\theta_{k}(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
for
k\in[1,A!]
.

(iii) As
W
-constraint =
odd-elegant
, thus each edge color set
\theta_{k}(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
for
k\in[1,A!]
.

Theorem 47
.

∗
For a fixed group of non-negative integers
a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{m}
with
A=\sum^{m}_{k=1}a_{k}\geq 1
, let
\textbf{{L}}(a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{m})
be the set of graphs in the lattice
\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
defined in Eq.(
195
) such that each connected
(p,q)
-graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{m})
holding
G=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{m}_{k=1}a_{k}T_{k}
. If each tree
T_{i}
in the lattice base
T
admits a
graceful
(k,d)
-total coloring

g_{i}
for
i\in[1,m]
, then each connected
(p,q)
-graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{m})
admits at least
A!
different
(k,d)
-graceful v-set e-proper colorings

\pi_{i}
with the edge color set

\pi_{i}(E(G))=\big\{k,k+d,\dots,k+(q-1)d\big\}=[k,k+(q-1)d]_{+d},~i\in[1,A!]

(201)

The main idea of the proof of Theorem
47
.
By Eq.(
196
), Eq.(
197
), Eq.(
199
) and Eq.(
200
) shown in the proof of Theorem
45
, we present the main proof process as follows:

Let
\Sigma_{j}(k,d)=k+d\sum^{j}_{k=1}q_{i_{k}}
for
j\in[0,A]
, where
\Sigma_{0}(k,d)=k
.

For
(k_{i_{1}},d_{i_{1}})=(k,d)
, we get the edge color set

g_{i_{1}}(E(H_{i_{1}}))=\big\{k,k+d,\dots,k+(q_{i_{1}}-1)d\big\}=[\Sigma_{0}(k,d),\Sigma_{1}(k,d)-d]_{+d}

As
(k_{i_{2}},d_{i_{2}})=(k+(q_{i_{1}}-1)d+d,d)=(\Sigma_{1}(k,d),d)
, we get the edge color set

{\begin{split}g_{i_{2}}(E(H_{i_{2}}))&=\big\{k+q_{i_{1}}d,k+q_{i_{1}}d+d,\dots,k+q_{i_{1}}d+(q_{i_{2}}-1)d\big\}\\
&=\big\{\Sigma_{1}(k,d),\Sigma_{1}(k,d)+d,\dots,\Sigma_{1}(k,d)+(q_{i_{2}}-1)d\big\}\\
&=[\Sigma_{1}(k,d),\Sigma_{2}(k,d)-d]_{+d}\end{split}}

In general, we take
(k_{i_{j+1}},d_{i_{j+1}})=(\Sigma_{j}(k,d),d)
, then we get the edge color set

{\begin{split}g_{i_{j+1}}(E(H_{i_{j+1}}))&=\big\{\Sigma_{j}(k,d),\Sigma_{j}(k,d)+d,\dots,\Sigma_{j}(k,d)+(q_{i_{j+1}}-1)d\big\}\\
&=[\Sigma_{j}(k,d),\Sigma_{j+1}(k,d)-d]_{+d}\end{split}}

For the last
(k_{i_{A}},d_{i_{A}})=(\Sigma_{A-1}(k,d),d)
, we have the edge color set

{\begin{split}g_{i_{A}}(E(H_{i_{A}}))&=\big\{\Sigma_{A-1}(k,d),\Sigma_{A-1}(k,d)+d,\dots,\Sigma_{A-1}(k,d)+(q_{i_{A}}-1)d\big\}\\
&=[\Sigma_{A-1}(k,d),\Sigma_{A}(k,d)-d]_{+d}\end{split}}

Thereby, based on the permutation
H_{i_{1}},H_{i_{2}},\dots,H_{i_{A}}
of the trees
b_{1}T_{1},b_{2}T_{2},\dots,b_{m}T_{m}
, the connected graph
G
admits a graceful v-set e-proper coloring
\pi_{i}=\langle g_{i_{1}},g_{i_{2}},\dots,g_{i_{A}}\rangle
defined by setting
g_{i_{j}}(x)\subseteq F_{i}(x)
,
g_{i_{j}}(y)\subseteq F_{i}(y)
and
F_{i}(xy)=g_{i_{j}}(xy)
if edge
xy\in E(H_{i_{j}})\subseteq E(G)
, such that the edge color set

{\begin{split}\pi_{i}(E(G))&=\big\{\pi_{i}(uv)=|\pi_{i}(u)-\pi_{i}(v)|:uv\in E(G)\big\}=\bigcup^{A}_{j=1}g_{i_{j}}(E(H_{i_{j}}))\\
&=\bigcup^{A}_{j=0}[\Sigma_{j}(k,d),\Sigma_{j+1}(k,d)-d]_{+d}\\
&=[k,k+(q-1)d]_{+d}\end{split}}

where
\pi_{i}(uv)=|\pi_{i}(u)-\pi_{i}(v)|
stands for
g_{i_{j}}(uv)=|g_{i_{j}}(u)-g_{i_{j}}(v)|
if edge
uv\in E(H_{i_{j}})
. Refer to the proof of Theorem
45
for the complete proof of Theorem
47
.
\square

Theorem 49
.

∗
For a fixed group of non-negative integers
d_{1},d_{2},\dots,d_{m}
with
\Phi=\sum^{m}_{k=1}d_{k}\geq 1
, let
\textbf{{L}}(d_{1},d_{2},\dots,d_{m})
be the set of graphs in the lattice
\textbf{{L}}([\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]Z^{0}\textbf{{T}})
defined in Eq.(
195
) such that each connected
(p,q)
-graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(d_{1},d_{2},\dots,d_{m})
holding
G=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{m}_{k=1}d_{k}T_{k}
. If each tree
T_{i}
of the lattice base
T
admits a
graceful
(k,d)
-total coloring

g_{i}
for
i\in[1,m]
, then each connected
(p,q)
-graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}(d_{1},d_{2},\dots,d_{m})
admits at least
\Phi!
different
magic-constraint v-set e-proper colorings

F_{i}
with
i\in[1,\Phi!]
.

Proof.

By Theorem
8
, each tree
T
with diameter
D(T)\geq 3
admits at least
2^{m}
different magic-constraint
(k,d)
-total colorings for magic-constraint
\{
edge-magic, edge-difference, felicitous-difference, graceful-difference
\}
, where
m+1=\Big\lceil\frac{D(T)}{2}\Big\rceil
.

Taking the
i
th permutation
G_{i_{1}},G_{i_{2}},\dots,G_{i_{\Phi}}
of the trees
d_{1}T_{1}
,
d_{2}T_{2}
,
\dots
,
d_{m}T_{m}
from the tree base
T
with
\Phi=\sum^{m}_{k=1}d_{k}\geq 1
, we have connected graphs

G=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{\Phi}_{k=1}G_{i_{k}}=[\odot_{\textrm{prop}}]^{m}_{k=1}d_{k}T_{k}

(203)

and each tree
G_{i_{j}}
admits a magic-constraint total coloring
\eta_{i_{j}}
related a constant
\lambda_{i_{j}}
.

Let
\beta_{i}=\Pi^{\Phi}_{k=1}\lambda_{i_{k}}
, and
\beta_{i_{j}}=\frac{\beta_{i}}{\lambda_{i_{j}}}
for
j\in[1,\Phi]
.

We define a new total coloring
h_{i_{j}}=\beta_{i_{j}}\eta_{i_{j}}
. For each edge
xy\in E(G_{i_{j}})
, then we have the edge-magic constraint

h_{i_{j}}(x)+h_{i_{j}}(y)+h_{i_{j}}(xy)=\beta_{i_{j}}[\eta_{i_{j}}(x)+\eta_{i_{j}}(y)+\eta_{i_{j}}(xy)]=\beta_{i_{j}}\lambda_{i_{j}}=\beta_{i}

(204)

when the edge-magic constraint
\eta_{i_{j}}(x)+\eta_{i_{j}}(y)+\eta_{i_{j}}(xy)=\lambda_{i_{j}}
holds true; and we have the edge-difference constraint

h_{i_{j}}(xy)+|h_{i_{j}}(x)-h_{i_{j}}(y)|=\beta_{i_{j}}[\eta_{i_{j}}(xy)+|\eta_{i_{j}}(x)-\eta_{i_{j}}(y)|]=\beta_{i_{j}}\lambda_{i_{j}}=\beta_{i}

(205)

when as the edge-difference constraint
\eta_{i_{j}}(xy)+|h_{i_{j}}(x)-\eta_{i_{j}}(y)|=\lambda_{i_{j}}
holds true; and we have the felicitous-difference constraint

|h_{i_{j}}(x)+h_{i_{j}}(y)-h_{i_{j}}(xy)|=\beta_{i_{j}}|\eta_{i_{j}}(x)+\eta_{i_{j}}(y)-\eta_{i_{j}}(xy)|=\beta_{i_{j}}\lambda_{i_{j}}=\beta_{i}

(206)

if the felicitous-difference constraint
|\eta_{i_{j}}(x)+\eta_{i_{j}}(y)-\eta_{i_{j}}(xy)|=\lambda_{i_{j}}
holds true; and we have the graceful-difference constraint

\big||h_{i_{j}}(x)-h_{i_{j}}(y)|-h_{i_{j}}(xy)\big|=\beta_{i_{j}}\big||\eta_{i_{j}}(x)-\eta_{i_{j}}(y)|-\eta_{i_{j}}(xy)\big|=\beta_{i_{j}}\lambda_{i_{j}}=\beta_{i}

(207)

as the graceful-difference constraint
\big||\eta_{i_{j}}(x)-\eta_{i_{j}}(y)|-\eta_{i_{j}}(xy)\big|=\lambda_{i_{j}}
holds true.

We define a
magic-constraint v-set e-proper coloring

F_{i}=\langle h_{i_{1}},h_{i_{2}},\dots,h_{i_{\Phi}}\rangle
with the constant
\beta_{i}
by setting
h_{i_{j}}(x)\subseteq F_{i}(x)
,
h_{i_{j}}(y)\subseteq F_{i}(y)
and
F_{i}(xy)=h_{i_{j}}(xy)
if edge
xy\in E(G_{i_{j}})
. Thereby, the theorem follows Eq.(
204
), Eq.(
205
), Eq.(
206
) and Eq.(
207
) for
i\in[1,\Phi!]
, since there are
\Phi!
permutations of the trees
d_{1}T_{1}
,
d_{2}T_{2}
,
\dots
,
d_{m}T_{m}
from the tree base
T
with
\Phi=\sum^{m}_{k=1}d_{k}\geq 1
.
∎

Corollary 50
.

∗
By the hypothesis and the proof of Theorem
49
and each magic-constraint v-set e-proper coloring

Theorem 51
.

By the hypothesis and the proof of Theorem
49
, there is a number-based string

s(\beta)=\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{M}

(212)

where
\beta_{i}=\lambda_{i_{1}}\lambda_{i_{2}}\cdots\lambda_{i_{\Phi}}
,
M=\Phi!
with
\Phi=\sum^{m}_{k=1}d_{k}\geq 1
. Moreover, there are:

(i) different number-based strings
s_{permu}(k)=\gamma_{k_{1}}\gamma_{k_{2}}\cdots\gamma_{k_{M}}
for
k\in[1,M!]
induced from all permutations
\gamma_{k_{1}},\gamma_{k_{2}},\cdots,\gamma_{k_{M}}
made by the numbers
\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\dots,\beta_{M}
.

(ii) different number-based strings
\beta_{i,t_{i}}=\rho^{t_{i}}_{j_{1}}\rho^{t_{i}}_{j_{2}}\cdots\rho^{t_{i}}_{j_{\Phi}}
with
t_{i}\in[1,\Phi!]
made by all permutations
\rho^{t_{i}}_{j_{1}},\rho^{t_{i}}_{j_{2}},\dots,\rho^{t_{i}}_{j_{\Phi}}
of the numbers
\lambda_{i_{1}},\lambda_{i_{2}},\dots,\lambda_{i_{\Phi}}
. Thereby, we get number-based strings

s(\beta_{t})=\beta_{1,t_{1}}\beta_{2,t_{2}}\cdots\beta_{M,t_{M}}

(213)

with more complex structure.

(iii) different number-based strings made by the combination of the above two cases.

Example 26
.

\textbf{{L}}(4am)

) such that each connected

.

Thereby, we get number-based strings
s_{j,r}=\beta_{j,r,1}\beta_{j,r,2}\cdots\beta_{j,r,a}
and

, where

and

.

-constraint v-set e-proper colorings

with

. Obviously, finding a particular connected graph

and the number-based strings
s_{j,1}s_{j,2}s_{j,3}s_{j,4}
made by the colorings admitted by the connected graph

seems to be quite difficult, even it is almost impossible.
\square

5.7
Number-based strings generated from indexed-colorings

In
[
35
]
the authors use maximal planar graphs to make various topological signature authentications. Here, we will use them to make number-based strings, since maximal planar graphs have many nice properties. In Wikipedia, the enumeration of planar graphs is as:

(i) The asymptotic for the number of
(labeled) planar graphs
on
n
vertices is
g\cdot n^{-7/2}\cdot n!\cdot\gamma^{n}
, where
\gamma\approx 27.22687
and
g\approx 0.43\times 10^{-5}
.

(ii) Almost all planar graphs have an exponential number of automorphisms.

(iii) The number of
unlabeled (non-isomorphic) planar graphs
on
n
vertices is between
27.2^{n}
and
30.06^{n}
, roughly, between
2^{4.7n}
and
2^{6n}
.

5.7.1
Maximal planar graphic lattices

A
maximal planar graph
is a connected planar graph with each face being a
triangle
.

Definition 46
.

For two vertex-disjoint maximal planar graphs
G
and
H
, we overlap a triangular face
\Delta_{abc}
of
G
with another triangular face
\Delta_{xyz}
of
H
into one triangular face

\Delta(abc\overline{\ominus}xyz)=\Delta_{abc}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]\Delta_{xyz}

by edge-coinciding

(i) two edges
ab,xy
into one edge
ab\overline{\ominus}xy
with
a\odot x
and
b\odot y
;

(ii) two edges
bc,yz
into one edge
bc\overline{\ominus}yz
with
b\odot y
; and

(iii) two edges
ca,zx
into one edge
ca\overline{\ominus}zx
with
c\odot z
and
x\odot a
.

The resultant graph is a maximal planar graph too, denoted as
G[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]H
, and we call the process of obtaining
G[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]H

triangular-face embedding operation
, and the maximal planar graph
G[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]H
is the result of
G
embedding
H
(resp.
H
embedding
G
).
\square

Remark 31
.

As the generalization of Definition
46
, we split a cycle
C
of
k
vertices in a maximal planar graph
G
in to two cycles
C\,^{\prime}
and
C\,^{\prime\prime}
, such that the cycle-split graph
G\wedge C
has just two vertex disjoint components
G^{C}_{out}
and
G^{C}_{in}
, called
semi-maximal planar graphs
(also
configurations

[
27
,
28
]
), where
G^{C}_{out}
contains the cycle
C\,^{\prime\prime}
and is in the
infinite plane
, and
G^{C}_{in}
contains the cycle
C\,^{\prime}
and is inside of
G
. So, we write
G=G^{C}_{out}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{k}]G^{C}_{in}
as the result of doing the cycle-coinciding operation
[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{k}]
to two semi-maximal planar graphs
G^{C}_{out}
and
G^{C}_{in}
.

We call a semi-maximal planar graph
G^{C}

configuration
, and the set
U^{*}
contains all configurations. Moreover, if any maximal planar graph
H
contains at least a configuration in
U^{*}
, we say that
U^{*}
is
unavoidable
. Assume that a maximal planar graph
H^{*}
of
n
vertices does not admit a proper 4-coloring, and any maximal planar graph
G
with
|V(G)|<|V(H^{*})|
is 4-colorable, then
H^{*}
is called a smallest counterexample of the Four-coloring Conjecture,
smallest FCC-counterexample
. If any smallest FCC-counterexample does not contain a configuration
G^{C}
, we say that the configuration
G^{C}
is
reducible
. If any maximal planar graph contains at least a configuration in
U^{*}
, and each configuration in
U^{*}
is reducible, then
U^{*}
is called a
unavoidable and reducible configuration set
.

As two semi-maximal planar graphs
G^{C}_{out}
and
G^{C}_{in}
in a maximal planar graph
G=G^{C}_{out}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{k}]G^{C}_{in}
approach to two infinite planes, then
G
becomes a sphere with an equatorial line
C
and each face to be a triangular face.
\parallel

Corollary 52
.

∗
Let
T^{C_{2k}}_{1},T^{C_{2k}}_{2},\dots,T^{C_{2k}}_{m}
be semi-maximal planar graphs with the cycle
C_{2k}
on
2k
vertices, then doing the cycle-coinciding operation
[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{2k}]
to them produces a
semi-maximal planar graph-book

[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{2k}]^{m}_{i=1}T^{C_{2k}}_{i}=T^{C_{2k}}_{1}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{2k}]T^{C_{2k}}_{2}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{2k}]T^{C_{2k}}_{2}\cdots[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{2k}]T^{C_{2k}}_{m}

with each
book-page

T^{C_{2k}}_{i}
and the
book-spine

C_{2k}
. Moreover, if each book-page
T^{C_{2k}}_{i}
admits a proper vertex
4
-coloring such that its own cycle
C_{2k}
is properly colored with two colors, then the semi-maximal planar graph-book
[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{2k}]^{m}_{i=1}T^{C_{2k}}_{i}
admits a proper vertex
4
-coloring too.

Example 27
.

In Fig.
24
, the graphs
P
,
B_{1}
,
B_{2}
and
B_{3}
are four semi-maximal planar graphs, such that each maximal planar graph
A_{i}=P[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{6}]B_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq 3
. Conversely, we split the cycle
C
of
k
vertices in each maximal planar graph
A_{i}
, the resultant graph
A_{i}\wedge C
has just two vertex disjoint semi-maximal planar graphs
P~(=G^{C}_{out}
, called
out-planar graph
) and
B_{i}~(=G^{C}_{in}
, called
inner-planar graph
) for
1\leq i\leq 3
.

In real application, we use the semi-maximal planar graph
P
as a
public-key graph
, and three semi-maximal planar graphs
B_{1}
,
B_{2}
and
B_{3}
are
private-key graphs
, thus, each maximal planar graph
A_{i}=P[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{6}]B_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq 3
is just a
topological signature authentication

A_{i}=A_{uth}\langle P,B_{i}\rangle
. Moreover, the Topcode-matrix
T_{code}(A_{i},f_{i})
of each maximal planar graph
A_{i}
admitting a
4
-coloring
f_{i}
can provide us
(3q_{i})!
different number-based strings, where
q_{i}=|E(A_{i})|
for
1\leq i\leq 3
.
\square

Figure 24:
Examples for the cycle-coinciding operation
[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{k}]
, cited from
[
35
]
.

1.Uncolored maximal planar graphic lattices.
Suppose that
\textbf{{P}}=(P_{1},P_{2},\dots,P_{n})
is a
maximal planar graph base
with mutually vertex-disjoint uncolored maximal planar graphs
P_{1},P_{2},\dots,P_{n}
holding
P_{i}\not\cong P_{j}
if
i\neq j
. Each graph
G=[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]^{n}_{k=1}a_{k}P_{k}
is a maximal planar graph obtained by doing the triangular-face embedding operation defined in Definition
46
on the maximal planar graphs
P_{1},P_{2},\dots,P_{n}
of the maximal planar graph base
P
, we get a
maximal planar graphic lattice
as follows

\textbf{{L}}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}})=\big\{[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]^{n}_{k=1}a_{k}P_{k}:a_{k}\in Z^{0},P_{k}\in\textbf{{P}}\big\}

(218)

with
\sum^{n}_{k=1}a_{k}\geq 1
.

However, there are many maximal planar graphs out of the maximal planar graphic lattices
\textbf{{L}}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}})
. Let
T_{i_{1}},T_{i_{2}},\dots,T_{i_{M}}
be a permutation of the maximal planar graphs
c_{1}P_{1},c_{2}P_{2}
,
\dots
,
c_{n}P_{n}
of the maximal planar graph base
P
, where with
M=\sum^{n}_{k=1}c_{k}\geq 1
.

For a maximal planar graph
H
, we do the triangular-face embedding operation to
H_{0}=H
and
T_{i_{1}}
, the resultant maximal planar graph is denoted as
H_{1}=H_{0}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]T_{i_{1}}
, next we get another maximal planar graph is denoted as
H_{2}=H_{1}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]T_{i_{2}}
, in general, we have maximal planar graphs

H_{k}=H_{k-1}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]T_{i_{k}},~k\in[1,M]

(219)

We write
H_{M}=H_{M-1}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]T_{i_{M}}=H[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]^{n}_{i=1}c_{i}P_{i}
, and get a maximal planar graphic lattice

\textbf{{L}}(S_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}})=\big\{H[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]^{n}_{i=1}c_{i}P_{i}:c_{i}\in Z^{0},P_{i}\in\textbf{{P}},H\in S_{mpg}\big\}

(220)

with
\sum^{n}_{k=1}c_{k}\geq 1
, where
S_{mpg}
is a set of maximal planar graphs.

Problem 21
.

About the lattices
\textbf{{L}}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}})
defined in Eq.(
218
),
\textbf{{L}}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})
defined in Eq.(
221
),
\textbf{{L}}(S_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}})
defined in Eq.(
220
) and
\textbf{{L}}(S^{c}_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})
defined in Eq.(
222
), we have the following questions:

(i) In a colored maximal planar graph
J^{c}=G^{c}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]H^{c}
, if
H^{c}
is not
4
-colorable, so is
J^{c}
. If there is a maximal planar graph that cannot be colored properly with 4 colors, then there are infinite maximal planar graphs that are not colored properly by 4 colors.

(ii) Suppose that a maximal planar graph base
P
contains all unavoidable and reducible configurations of maximal planar graphs. Is there a maximal planar graph
G^{*}=G[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]T
with a maximal planar graph
T
and
G\in\textbf{{L}}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}})
, or
G\in\textbf{{L}}(S_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}})
, such that
G^{*}
contains no any one of the known unavoidable and reducible configurations of maximal planar graphs? For example, using the triangular-face embedding operation or other technique to destroy as many configurations as we can along the way in the process of making
G^{*}
.

(iii) For a
maximal planar graph sequence

G_{n+1}=G_{n}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]H_{n}
with
n\geq 1
, each
H_{n}
is a maximal planar graph and
G_{1}\in\textbf{{L}}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}})
, or
G_{1}\in\textbf{{L}}(S_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}})
. As
n\rightarrow\infty
, the maximal planar graph sequence
\{G_{n+1}\}
induces a
xOy
-plane titled by maximal planar graphs, we call it
triangular-plane

\Delta_{xOy}
. In this construction, the
boundary
of the triangular-plane
\Delta_{xOy}
is a
triangle
. Does the triangular-plane
\Delta_{xOy}
contains all configurations of maximal planar graphs?

2. Colored maximal planar graphic lattices.
If a colored maximal planar graph
G^{c}
admits a proper vertex coloring
f
and another colored maximal planar graph
H^{c}
admits a proper vertex coloring
g
, and
f(a)=g(x)
,
f(b)=g(y)
and
f(c)=g(z)
in tow triangular faces
\Delta_{abc}\subset G^{c}
and
\Delta_{xyz}\subset H^{c}
, we have a colored maximal planar graph
G^{c}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]H^{c}
admitting a proper vertex coloring
F=f[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]g
by the triangular-face embedding operation defined in Definition
46
, distinguishingly, we call the process of obtaining the colored maximal planar graph
G^{c}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]H^{c}

colored triangular-face embedding operation
.

Let
\textbf{{P}}^{c}=(P^{c}_{1},P^{c}_{2},\dots,P^{c}_{n})
be a
colored maximal planar graph base
with mutually disjoint maximal planar graphs
P^{c}_{1},P^{c}_{2},\dots,P^{c}_{n}
holding
P^{c}_{i}\not\cong P^{c}_{j}
if
i\neq j
, and each maximal planar graph
P^{c}_{j}
admits a proper vertex coloring
g_{j}
with
j\in[1,n]
. Doing the colored triangular-face embedding operation defined above to the mutually disjoint maximal planar graphs
b_{1}P^{c}_{1},b_{2}P^{c}_{2},\dots,b_{n}P^{c}_{n}
for
\sum^{n}_{k=1}b_{k}\geq 1
, then we get colored maximal planar graphs
G=[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]^{n}_{k=1}b_{k}P^{c}_{k}
admitting proper vertex coloring induced by the proper vertex colorings
g_{1},g_{2}\dots,g_{n}
, immediately, the following set

\textbf{{L}}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})=\big\{[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]^{n}_{k=1}b_{k}P^{c}_{k}:b_{k}\in Z^{0},~P^{c}_{k}\in\textbf{{P}}^{c}\big\}

(221)

with
\sum^{n}_{k=1}b_{k}\geq 1
is called
colored maximal planar graphic lattice
.

Theorem 53
.

∗

Coloring closure.
If each maximal planar graph
P^{c}_{j}
of a colored maximal planar graph base
\textbf{{P}}^{c}
admits a proper vertex
4
-coloring, then each maximal planar graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})
defined in Eq.(
221
) admits a proper vertex
4
-coloring too.

Let
S^{c}_{mpg}
be a set of colored maximal planar graphs. By the way similarly with that of obtaining the maximal planar graphic lattice
\textbf{{L}}(S_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}})
, we get a colored maximal planar graphic lattice

\textbf{{L}}(S^{c}_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})=\big\{H^{c}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]^{n}_{i=1}d_{i}P^{c}_{i}:d_{i}\in Z^{0},~P^{c}_{i}\in\textbf{{P}}^{c},~H^{c}\in S^{c}_{mpg}\big\}

(222)

with
\sum^{n}_{i=1}d_{i}\geq 1
.

Theorem 54
.

∗

Coloring closure.
If each maximal planar graph
P^{c}_{j}
in a colored maximal planar graph base
\textbf{{P}}^{c}
admits a proper vertex
4
-coloring, and each maximal planar graph
H\in S^{c}_{mpg}
admits a proper vertex
4
-coloring, then each maximal planar graph of the colored maximal planar graphic lattice
\textbf{{L}}(S^{c}_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})
defined in Eq.(
222
) admits a proper vertex
4
-coloring too.

Remark 32
.

In
[
27
]
, Prof. Xu mentioned: There are many variations of the Euler’s formula, which play an essential role in the development of discharging, the key approach for exploiting the computer-assisted proof of the Four-Color Conjecture by investigating the unavoidability and reducibility of some configurations of maximal planar graphs
[
16
]
. Haken and Appel spent seven years investigating configurations in more details and eventually in 1976 (with the help of Koch and about 1200 hour of fast mainframe computer) gave a computer-based proof of the Four-Color Conjecture (Ref.
[
17
]
and
[
18
]
), where the number of discharging rules and the number of
unavoidable configurations
they used are 487 and 1936, respectively.

In the research process of the Four-Color Conjecture, many challenges are encountered inevitably, and as a result new conjectures are proposed accordingly, such as Uniquely Four Chromatic planar graphs conjecture.
\parallel

Let
M_{PG}(k)
be a maximal planar graph containing the
k
th unavoidable configuration of 1936 unavoidable configurations
[
27
]
, and the vertex number
|V(M_{PG}(k))|
is the smallest one in all maximal planar graphs containing the
k
th unavoidable configuration. Then we have a
MPG-configuration graphic lattice

\textbf{{L}}([\overline{\ominus}]Z^{0}\textbf{{C}}_{\textrm{onfi}})=\big\{[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]^{1936}_{k=1}a_{k}M_{PG}(k):~a_{k}\in Z^{0},~M_{PG}(k)\in\textbf{{C}}_{\textrm{onfi}}\big\}

(223)

with the
configuration graph base

\textbf{{C}}_{\textrm{onfi}}=(M_{PG}(1),M_{PG}(2),\dots,M_{PG}(1936))
.

Let
S_{mpg}
be the set of maximal planar graphs. Do cycle-coinciding operation to a inner face and a maximal planar graph
M_{PG}(k)\in S_{mpg}
, the resultant maximal planar graph is denoted as
G[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]^{1936}_{k=1}a_{k}M_{PG}(k)
, then we have a
MPG-configuration graphic lattice

\textbf{{L}}(S_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}]Z^{0}\textbf{{C}}_{\textrm{onfi}})=\big\{G[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]^{1936}_{k=1}b_{k}M_{PG}(k):~G\in S_{mpg},~b_{k}\in Z^{0},~M_{PG}(k)\in\textbf{{C}}_{\textrm{onfi}}\big\}

(224)

It is necessary to prove that there are only 1936 unavoidable configurations on the maximum planar graphs for determining whether the graphs in two MPG-configuration graphic lattices
\textbf{{L}}([\overline{\ominus}]Z^{0}\textbf{{C}}_{\textrm{onfi}})
and
\textbf{{L}}(S_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}]Z^{0}\textbf{{C}}_{\textrm{onfi}})
are 4-color colorable.

Theorem 55
.

Using maximal planar graphs titles the
xOy
-plane, such that the boundary of the triangular-plane
\Delta_{xOy}
is a triangle.

Remark 33
.

If it is allowed that the results of the operation
[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]^{n}_{k=1}b_{k}P^{c}_{k}
in
\textbf{{L}}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})
defined in Eq.(
221
) are not necessarily maximal planar graphs, then we get a graphic lattice
\textbf{{L}}^{*}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})
holding

\textbf{{L}}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})\subset\textbf{{L}}^{*}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})
.

Similarly, the results of the operation
H^{c}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]^{n}_{i=1}d_{i}P^{c}_{i}
in
\textbf{{L}}(S^{c}_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})
defined in Eq.(
222
) are not necessarily maximal planar graphs, then we get a graphic lattice
\textbf{{L}}^{*}(S^{c}_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})
holding
\textbf{{L}}(S^{c}_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})\subset\textbf{{L}}^{*}(S^{c}_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})
.

Notice that
determining
whether a graph
G\in\textbf{{L}}^{*}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})
or
G\in\textbf{{L}}^{*}(S^{c}_{mpg}[\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})
to be a maximal planar graph is NP-complete.
\parallel

.

∗
Each graph of two graphic lattices
\textbf{{L}}^{*}([\overline{\ominus}^{cyc}_{3}]Z^{0}\textbf{{P}}^{c})
and

admits a proper vertex

and Theorem
